# TraceSetu: complete solution, project skill and SIH handover

**Edition:** 3 October 2026, Asia/Calcutta — replacement presentation and refreshed primary-source review; earlier branding, real-case and implementation records preserved. **Product:** TraceSetu (Trace + Setu / सेतु), formerly Vittanvaya; originally Custody Atlas. **Audience:** an AI or developer with no prior conversation. **Document form:** one self-contained file, as explicitly requested by the user. It contains the original requirements, current implementation, complete recorded research report, source register, operating instructions, actual contracts, verification evidence and future backlog. Supporting source files remain the executable authority; this skill is not a replacement repository or a backup of private case data.

<a id="current-0"></a>

### Concrete forensic-method review and revised PPT — 29 September 2026

The user challenged the absence of a concrete attribution method and requested research papers, an unchanged-design PPT revision and a simple explanatory paper. Their corrected scope is the original cryptocurrency nearest-VASP problem, **not ATM withdrawal prediction**. They explicitly selected `output/submission/TraceSetu_ANANTHA_SIH2026.pptx`. The source remains unchanged. The revised copy is `output/submission/TraceSetu_ANANTHA_SIH2026_Research_Revision.pptx`: six slides, same shape geometry, imagery, themes, font properties and three native tables. Content now distinguishes current MVP from proposed inference; references and speaker notes explain assumptions. Final native PowerPoint rendering and package/layout checks passed. Local prototype and private GitHub links remain correctly labelled. No AI-generated graphics or watermark were added.

**Research method:** seeded service/deposit discovery plus time-respecting first-custody tracing. A separate observed-transfer graph must not be confused with service-control assertions. The named operator comes from externally sourced anchors; no structural method can invent a legal company name. Account-based token sweep/gas patterns can generate unconfirmed deposit hypotheses; Bitcoin shared-input/change/peel extensions need chain-specific guards. Source-family exclusion, temporal validity and missing coverage must survive recomputation. Unknown is an explicit result. An ordinary customer can exactly imitate the proposed public pattern, so even a full match is not proof of control.

**Reference implementation only:** `research/method-review-20260929/reference/` contains `method.py`, fictional fixtures, tests, `run_experiment.py`, README and actual `results.json`. **64 synthetic tests passed**, independently rerun. Reviews corrected address-only pruning of later arrivals, stale event/anchor assessment reuse and service-gas role stopping. The rule requires complete attested activity/receipts, zero opening balances, exact isolated token cycles, sourced gas-feeder/hot roles, fee and closing-balance reconciliation. Three cycles and 100 integer units are toy parameters, not calibrated research thresholds. The snapshot digest binds supplied events/anchors only; changed coverage/balances/policy require rerunning detection. Inference is retrospective at the declared cutoff; later cycles may support an earlier candidate. General trace paths establish chronological connectivity, not same-unit provenance. Native residual-gas returns are excluded; this is not a reproduction of the full Evonax sequence.

**Measured synthetic comparison:** existing-label baseline reaches a hot-wallet assertion at two hops; complete pattern stops at a one-hop provisional deposit hypothesis. Missing coverage or required sources retains an unresolved boundary. Perfect customer imitation remains indistinguishable. These are fictional outcomes, not live exchange attribution or precision/recall. No new detector was integrated into the app, no UI was changed, no official SAHYOG access exists, and no real notices/transfers were made. The full-product backlog remains paused. This task does not authorise GitHub publication of the historical workspace.

**Deliverables:** `output/pdf/TraceSetu_Forensic_Method_Research_Paper.pdf` (eight visually reviewed pages), editable `research/method-review-20260929/TraceSetu_Forensic_Method_Research_Paper.md`, revised PPT above, and the executable reference. Paper covers the exact title, plain-language problem, two-graph example, detector, Bitcoin/temporal algorithms, actual status, evaluation baselines/metrics, all PS requirement groups and ten primary research references. Read the appended “29 September 2026 method-review paper” for the complete self-contained text. The implementation checklist records this separate research milestone; named production task counts remain **52/95**, with 43 remaining. No confidence, novelty, efficiency or recovery superiority is claimed.

### New agent: the essential product context

**TraceSetu is an investigation workbench for authorised teams dealing with an unknown cryptocurrency wallet.** It follows recorded transfers to the first supported receiving service on each path, explains the evidence and missing coverage, and helps prepare a reviewed next action. It does not identify a beneficial owner from an address or itself freeze assets.

**Primary users:** investigating officers, technical analysts, independent reviewers/supervisors and authorised disclosure staff, supported by agency administrators. VASP compliance teams are intended external counterparties; victims are indirect beneficiaries. SIH judges evaluate the prototype. These personas are not all separate implemented account roles. The app's roles are investigator, reviewer and admin; details and their current/future boundaries are in section 20.

**Product proposition:** a defensible first-custody answer plus an inspectable path to the next evidence decision. Five core mechanisms are the bounded custody frontier, nearestness certificate, source-family challenge, budget-aware query execution and independently reviewed service feedback. Signed evidence/replay supports them. The mechanisms are demonstrated; comparative superiority and real-world accuracy are not established.

**Current delivery:** a working independent SIH MVP with a classic light UI, complete in-app guide, controlled synthetic end-to-end exercises and a verified bounded real Bitcoin acquisition/refresh. Official SAHYOG and broad live ownership attribution remain unavailable. The full production solution is documented as future scope. The user has authorized the real public-case recording; use section 21 for the current TraceSetu deliverables.

### Essential facts an agent must not get wrong

- The app and algorithm work on explicit synthetic training cases and strict imported snapshots. Six read-only chain adapters are implemented and parser-tested. **A bounded live Bitcoin sample, browser refresh, exact output amounts, raw response hash and signed live-bundle replay were verified on 25 September 2026. Broader multi-chain coverage and real-world VASP ownership attribution remain unvalidated.** No paid intelligence credentials or official SAHYOG contract were supplied.
- **Official SAHYOG is not connected. No real disclosure notice was sent. No assets were frozen.** Request exports deliberately say `NOT_SENT` and `NOT_CONNECTED`.
- A transaction edge, an address label, a service's legal identity and a beneficial owner are different facts. Public history alone is not a VASP ownership database.
- Unknown is not low risk, self-custody or proof that no VASP exists. Nearest within a bounded acquired graph is not nearest in the real world.
- The demo does **not** fully satisfy every production requirement in the statement. The matrix below names the gaps. Never treat 12/12 scoped MVP checks as 100% problem-statement fulfillment.
- The actual running stack is Python/FastAPI/SQLAlchemy/SQLite WAL plus React/TypeScript/Cytoscape and local content-addressed objects. Temporal, Neo4j, S3 Object Lock, agency OIDC/MFA and large-scale deployment appear in the future architecture; they are not running components of this MVP.
- No LLM, machine learning model, embedding search or invented AI confidence engine is required or implemented for attribution. Rules and graph traversal are deterministic. The word “intelligence” describes evidence processing, not an oracle.
- Passwords, `.env` secrets, session cookies, private signing keys and private investigation data must not be embedded in this shareable skill. Local bootstrap accounts are `investigator`, `reviewer`, `admin`; their generated passwords are in `.local/bootstrap-credentials.txt`. That location is sufficient for an authorized local operator.
- Research quotations in this file are historical project-authored material, not fresh legal advice or current supplier guarantees. Recheck official primary sources when implementing time-sensitive API/legal changes.

## 1. Exact user-supplied problem statement

The following is the exact title and description wording supplied by the user. Only the table layout and HTML line-break markup are converted to readable text. The separate organisational metadata above came from the user's clarification. The supplied description is the requirements baseline, not a claim that its proposed integration already exists.

<!-- exact-problem-statement:start -->

```text
Automated Attribution of Unknown Cryptocurrency Wallets to Nearest Virtual Asset Service Providers (VASPs) through Blockchain Intelligence APIs

• Background The rapid adoption of Virtual Digital Assets (VDAs) and decentralized blockchain ecosystems has significantly increased the complexity of cybercrime investigations globally. Law Enforcement Agencies (LEAs) frequently encounter cryptocurrency wallet addresses linked to cyber frauds, ransomware, investment scams, darknet activities, and laundering of crime proceeds.

Under the existing investigation workflow, LEAs raise lawful information disclosure requests through the SAHYOG Portal to Virtual Asset Service Providers (VASPs) such as crypto exchanges, custodial wallet providers, and trading platforms. However, in many cases, the suspect wallet identified during investigations belongs to an unhosted wallet or a wallet for which the associated VASP is unknown. This creates major delays in attribution, freezing of assets, and identification of the beneficial owner.

Blockchain transactions generally pass through multiple intermediary wallets before reaching centralized exchanges. Identifying the 'nearest direct deposit accepting exchange' manually through blockchain analysis is time-consuming and requires specialized expertise.

• Description The proposed system envisages development of an Automated Blockchain Intelligence & VASP Attribution Engine integrated with the SAHYOG Portal through APIs.

**The system should:**

• Automatically analyze suspect cryptocurrency wallet addresses reported during investigations on the Sahyog Platform
• Automatically trace blockchain transaction paths to identify:

o nearest centralized exchange, o custodial wallet service, o or VASP receiving direct deposits from the suspect wallet.

• Map blockchain of deposit addresses and transaction flows across multiple blockchain networks such as:

o Bitcoin, o Ethereum, o Tron, o BNB Chain, o Solana, o Polygon o and other major chains.

• Support identification of:

o exchange clusters, o hot wallets, o deposit wallets, o mixers/tumblers, o DeFi bridges, o and cross-chain swap services.

• Integrate Sahyog with blockchain intelligence APIs and graph analytics engines.
• Provide automated tagging and confidence scoring for suspected VASPs.
• Generate investigation-ready reports for LEAs.
• Assist investigators in automatically routing lawful disclosure or freezing requests to the correct VASP through the SAHYOG Portal.

**The system may additionally support:**

• visualization of fund movement,
• cross-chain transaction mapping,
• risk scoring,
• identification of laundering typologies,
• and alerting for high-risk wallets linked to ransomware, darknet, terrorism financing, or fraud ecosystems.
• Expected Solution A software-based blockchain intelligence platform integrated with the SAHYOG ecosystem capable of:
• Automated identification of nearest VASP/exchange linked to unknown wallets.
• API-driven blockchain tracing and attribution support.
• Multi-chain transaction analysis and visualization.
• Real-time generation of investigative intelligence.
• Risk classification of wallets and transaction flows.
• Dashboard for LEAs with case-based analytics and reporting.
• Scalable architecture capable of handling large-volume blockchain transaction analysis.

**The solution should aim to:**

• reduce investigation time,
• improve asset freezing efficiency,
• enhance attribution capabilities,
• and strengthen cross-border cybercrime investigations involving VDAs
```

<!-- exact-problem-statement:end -->

## 2. Requirement-by-requirement implementation and demo coverage

Read the status precisely: **demonstrated locally** means a working local workflow/test; **implemented, live unvalidated** means code exists but real endpoint acceptance does not; **partial** means only the named subset works; **external/deferred** means no completed implementation/acceptance claim. A screenshot or synthetic path never certifies live attribution.

| ID | Requirement from the statement | Current behavior and implementation | Demo / verification | Remaining boundary |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| R01 | Automatically analyse suspect wallets reported on SAHYOG | Case intake, validated chain/address/window, authenticated analysis API and durable jobs in `main.py`, `worker.py` | Investigator creates/runs a case; API and browser tests | Official SAHYOG inbound contract/authentication not available; local intake is not SAHYOG ingestion |
| R02 | Identify nearest centralized exchange | `engine.py` follows chronological directed transfers and stops at first custody boundary per path | Alpha one hop, Beta two hops; nearestness and shallow-gap tests | Only source-qualified labels can establish a candidate; real nearest VASP remains unproven |
| R03 | Identify nearest custodial wallet/VASP receiving deposits | Exchange/custodian categories; sourced deposit role; direct and multi-hop candidates | Deposit-role assertion visible in candidate/report | “Receiving funds”, “operated by exchange” and “accepts customer deposits” are separate claims |
| R04 | Bitcoin | Esplora history, input sets, outputs, spent outpoints, exact integer amounts | Synthetic parser/outpoint tests | Live access, reorg/pinned head, scripts without addresses, ownership/change/CoinJoin clustering pending |
| R05 | Ethereum | Etherscan V2 normal/internal indexes and receipt/block-reconciled standard ERC20 transfer logs | Receipt, ordering, missing/removed-log and partial-coverage tests | Key/entitlement and live checks absent; native/internal reconciliation and asset registry incomplete |
| R06 | Tron | Confirmed native/TRC20 history, fingerprint pagination, non-transfer exclusion | Synthetic TronGrid tests | Internal/event-order/finality coverage and live acceptance incomplete |
| R07 | BNB Chain | EVM adapter, independent chain identity/chain ID 56 | Adapter and chain-qualified identity tests | EVM compatibility does not certify this network's live coverage or finality |

| R11 | Exchange clusters | Assertion role `cluster` can represent sourced intelligence | Schema, review and inspector | Automatic cluster inference/reversible membership mechanism not implemented |
| R12 | Hot/deposit wallets | Assertion roles `hot`, `deposit`, `unknown`, source family and validity interval | Candidate/label inspection; boundary stops before synthetic Alpha hot-wallet sweep | Roles must be evidenced; graph patterns alone do not identify beneficial owners |
| R13 | Mixers/tumblers | Sourced mixer label stops deterministic traversal and contributes a sourced risk signal | Synthetic mixer boundary and challenge tests | No invented deposit-to-withdrawal linkage or mixer deanonymisation |
| R14 | DeFi bridges | CCTP V2 native USDC, Ethereum ↔ Polygon proof verification and review | Separate bridge scenario, receipts/signatures/mint, challenge/withdrawal/export | Only registered protocol/contracts; generic DeFi bridge support not implemented |
| R15 | Cross-chain swap services | DeFi/bridge service assertions and explicit unresolved asset transitions | Unknown transitions can be shown as gaps | General DEX/aggregator/opaque cross-chain swap decoding is not implemented |

Categories are not a probability or complete fulfillment of numeric scoring |
| R20 | Investigation-ready LEA reports | PDF/JSON/CSV/raw artifacts, signed ZIP, verifier, audit excerpt | Export, tamper tests, browser replay and rendered PDF | Agency acceptance, trusted signing identity and legal admissibility require institutional review |

| R22 | Fund-movement visualization (optional) | Interactive directed graph, wallet/transfer inspector, explicit Bitcoin transaction nodes | Graph browser checks and mobile layout | Layout is not evidence of ownership; mixed path upper bounds must not be summed |

| R24 | Risk scoring/classification | Sourced risk tags and `tagged_exposure`/`unassessed` | Synthetic mixer exposure and report | No calibrated numeric risk score; no criminality conclusion |
| R25 | Laundering typologies (optional) | Research specifies transparent rules and false-positive controls | Research only | Dedicated typology rule engine not implemented |
| R26 | High-risk alerts (optional) | Local wallet watches and in-case change alerts with budgets and duplicate/closed-case checks | Scheduler and workflow tests | No connected ransomware/darknet/terrorism/fraud intelligence corpus or external notification delivery |

| R30 | Reduce investigation time, improve freezing efficiency, strengthen cross-border work | Reproducible first-custody result, focused next-query workflow and reviewed recipient package support these goals | Concrete workflow exists | Time/cost/accuracy/asset-action benefits remain unmeasured; legal authority and VASP cooperation external |

**If asked “does our demo fulfill everything?”:** answer that it addresses every requirement in a traceable design and demonstrates the implemented workflow, but several items are partial, deferred or externally gated. Do not quietly change “required” into “optional”, call a stub operational, or promise all requirements have been solved. If the user later wants the missing MVP features, propose and implement a bounded next increment with explicit simulated/live distinctions, then update this matrix after tests.

## 4. Product proposition, USP and defensible efficiency

**Pitch:** “Find the first evidenced receiving VASP, explain why it is the nearest supported answer, and show exactly what evidence could change that answer.”

| Mechanism | Investigator benefit | Implemented proof | What must still be measured |
|---|---|---|---|
| First-custody frontier | Avoid bypassing a nearer service to name a famous distant exchange | Engine tests stop each branch, retain gaps/conflicts, and never cross a custodial private ledger | Real first-service precision and missed-service rate |
| Nearestness certificate | Make bounded answer and missing evidence inspectable | Snapshot/spec/version binding, shallow-gap logic, offline replay | Real data/identity coverage |
| Source-family challenge | Reveal dependence on a disputed label or bridge source | Remove source and recompute; preserve original; expose newly unacquired branches | Investigator decision quality/time |
| Budgeted next query | Focus limited request capacity on unresolved frontiers that can matter | Versioned depth-first priority, estimated calls, selected execution with hard cap across retry | Cost-matched held-out advantage over baselines; vendor billing rather than only HTTP counts |
| Reviewed signed service feedback | Convert scoped authenticated answers into controlled reusable case evidence | Request/key/hash binding, independent promotion, withdrawal/stale guards | Live interoperability, trusted identity onboarding, permissioned cross-case reuse |
| Portable evidence | Reproduce a result when labels/providers change | Hash/signature/membership checks plus deterministic replay | Agency key governance, licensed retention and legal acceptance |

The current planner protects shallower gaps before same-depth utility. Its endpoint floors are Bitcoin 1, Ethereum/BNB/Polygon 3, Tron 2, Solana 1. Estimated cost is the maximum of this floor and prior pages plus one if a cursor remains. Same-depth ordering uses exact `Fraction(distinct_observed_paths, estimated_calls)`, with deterministic tie breaks. A deferred shallow affordable/availability decision is not a probability; deeper budget selection is constrained by a shallow budget barrier. Manual evidence reviews and unavailable providers remain distinct rows.

Practical cost controls already present: bounded acquisition, no fetch beyond a known custody/mixer boundary, within-acquisition caching, and durable saved-response reuse during query-job recovery. The explicit training path requires zero paid API calls. Preserved imports can support a lawful offline demonstration. None establishes a percentage saving. Do not scrape prohibited data, bypass subscriptions, pretend an endpoint is free, or assume batching reduces per-address billing.

Competition research is a review of public supplier descriptions, not a hands-on feature audit. Chainalysis Reactor/Rapid, TRM Triage, Elliptic Investigator, GraphSense and Iknaio advertise overlapping capabilities. The detailed comparison and original URLs are embedded below. Do not claim nobody else has these features or guarantee winning SIH.

For a later measured efficiency claim: use the same authorised, entity/time-disjoint dataset and provider entitlements for chronological BFS, fixed-depth and budget-planner runs; match actual cost budgets; record correct first-service precision, coverage, abstention, false routes, latency, billable units/HTTP, and investigator time. Retain ambiguous/negative cases, bootstrap confidence intervals and ablations. Vendor output cannot serve as its own independent ground truth. The original report's thresholds and timetable are proposals, not achieved measurements.

## 5. Actual architecture, stack and repository map

```text
Browser: React + TypeScript + Cytoscape
    | session cookie + CSRF; own authenticated case APIs
FastAPI application
    | SQLAlchemy transactions                | content-addressed bytes
SQLite WAL (tested) / PostgreSQL (unvalidated)   .local/objects/<prefix>/<sha256>
    | durable Job records + leases           | snapshots/raws/reports/signing material
Local worker thread
    | read-only bounded acquisition OR imported/explicit fixture snapshot
Provider adapters -> normalized Snapshot -> deterministic custody engine
    |                           |                         |
reviewed assertions/proofs     coverage + gaps          candidates + certificate
    |                           |                         |
superseding reassessment / query execution -> dashboard / PDF / signed ZIP
    |
reviewed recipient -> payload-bound request approval -> NOT_SENT export
    |
request/key-bound signed service response -> independent review -> case assertion
```

The Python traversal is the graph analytics engine; Cytoscape is the visualization library. There is no Neo4j projection in the running MVP. SQLAlchemy creates tables at startup with `Base.metadata.create_all`; versioned migration tooling is not implemented. PostgreSQL URL/driver availability is not evidence of concurrency/rollback testing.

Recorded lock versions include Python 3.12 runtime, FastAPI 0.141.1, Uvicorn 0.53.0, SQLAlchemy 2.0.54, Pydantic 2.13.5, pydantic-settings 2.15.0, httpx 0.28.1, cryptography 50.0.1, eth-utils 6.0.0, eth-keys 0.7.0, ReportLab 5.0.1, psycopg 3.3.6, pytest 9.1.1 and Ruff 0.16.8. ## 6. Evidence model and exact algorithm behavior

The complete current Python contracts are embedded below. `Contract` rejects extra fields. Snapshots have an explicit mode (`fixture`, `imported`, `live`), origin, acquisition window, events, assertions, coverage, optional bridge proofs and optional reconciliation gaps. Validate chain-specific addresses for nonfixture snapshots. Do not broaden the requested trace window beyond the acquired window.

Important limits: at most 20,000 events and 20,000 assertions per snapshot, 5,000 coverage scopes, 100 bridge proofs, 20,000 reconciliation gaps. `TraceSpec` permits 1–8 hops (default 3), 10–10,000 states (default 2,000), 1–200 requests (default 30), explicit start/end, optional asset and exact integer-string minimum amount. The engine caps transitions at `min(200000, max_states * 20)`. These are resource limits, not claims of database capacity or completeness.

Transfers preserve ID, chain, transaction ID, senders, recipient, asset, integer-string amount, decimals, timestamp, optional `[block, transaction_index, event_index]`, finality, success, kind, evidence and Bitcoin/cross-chain linkage. Account transfers have one sender; Bitcoin UTXO events preserve the input set and output index. Asset identity includes chain. Native units are not floats, fiat values or incident allocations.

Assertions carry exact wallet/chain, entity, category (`exchange`, `custodian`, `mixer`, `bridge`, `defi`, `unknown`), role (`deposit`, `hot`, `cluster`, `contract`, `unknown`), evidence grade (`reviewed`, `provider`, `hypothesis`), source family/source/evidence, validity interval and risk tags. Snapshot uniqueness rejects duplicate IDs/scopes; normalization must not create duplicate scopes. Empty bridge/hold extensions are omitted to preserve canonical hashes of older snapshots.

Algorithm operation:

1. Check the scope fits the snapshot. Validate eligible bridge proofs; duplicate source-domain/nonce packages or failed/excluded/hypothesis proofs produce unresolved frontiers.
2. Construct outgoing events and source-qualified labels. Withhold event IDs covered by persistent reconciliation gaps, including proof-derived events with such IDs. Sort outgoing observations deterministically by time, ledger position and ID.
3. Traverse separate chronological path states from the seed. Preserve splits and alternate asset histories; do not merge histories into invented fungible taint.
4. Evaluate labels valid at arrival time. Custody labels stop the branch, including hypotheses/conflicts. Non-hypothesis disagreement on entity/category is a conflict. Mixer boundaries stop deterministic continuation. Known custody is not an invitation to follow arbitrary withdrawals.
5. Record missing/partial/unavailable history, holds, hop/state/transition limits and unsupported transitions explicitly. No hidden pruning can become a completeness claim.
6. Ignore unsuccessful, pending, orphaned, out-of-window and already-used events. Same-second account causality needs ledger positions and increasing order. Bitcoin continuation requires a previously observed output's actual outpoint among the next inputs; same address alone is insufficient. Exact outpoint linkage has specific chronology handling in the code.
7. Keep one asset history unless a supported verified protocol transition explains the change. Other transitions remain unresolved. Apply the declared minimum amount without claiming filtered funds disappeared.
8. For CCTP, use the verified net-mint protocol edge and set subsequent destination traversal's time/position to the actual destination mint. Each such link counts as one protocol-correlated hop.
9. Return candidates, source assertions, paths, graph events/nodes, all frontiers, risk signals, query queue and a certificate. Candidate order is hop count, evidenced-status preference, entity and path; it is not a likelihood ranking.
10. Per-path possible exposure upper bound is the minimum relevant transfer amount; lower bound is zero. Overlapping bounds must never be summed as traced criminal proceeds or recovered funds.

`nearest_known_target_in_snapshot_proven` is true only when an evidenced minimum exists and no unresolved shallower frontier can change that bounded known-target answer. `nearest_real_vasp_proven` remains false: unlabelled intermediaries may themselves be custodial. A mixer gap at the same/deeper depth can coexist with a proven nearer known target while overall `complete` remains false. Explain these different fields instead of treating the word “proven” as global completeness.

Source challenge recomputes after excluding assertion/proof IDs or provenance families. It reports added/removed candidates and whether a newly open branch requires expansion. This is sensitivity analysis, not a probability, causal guilt determination or independent corroboration of all provider sources.

<a id="current-7"></a>

## 7. Acquisition, chain coverage and query execution

Acquisition is read-only and bounded: configured timeout (default 15 seconds), roughly 0.55-second minimum spacing, 8 MB maximum provider response body, hard request cap, raw-response SHA-256 references and partial-response checkpoints. Requests do not transfer funds, submit bridge messages or send legal notices. The same HTTP transport is reused by the protocol adapter. Cache keys are digests of request identity; credential-bearing request URLs/parameters are not persisted as readable metadata.

- Bitcoin defaults to the Esplora-compatible `https://mempool.space/api`; Blockstream's Esplora documentation defines the interface. Confirmed pages preserve input addresses/outpoints and outputs. The provider's representation of history is not an independently proven canonical chain or ownership cluster.
- EVM uses Etherscan V2 for Ethereum/BNB/Polygon chain IDs 1/56/137. Normal/internal index coverage remains partial. Standard ERC20 rows trigger receipt and block verification of success, transaction/block identity, position, timestamp and exact log semantics. Removed/inconsistent/missing receipt evidence cannot fall back to an unverified index edge. Token decimals/legitimacy still require stronger asset validation.
- Optional Etherscan nametags are disabled by default and require actual entitlement. Metadata is a hypothesis, not historical ownership, an exact legal entity or proof of direct-deposit acceptance. Documentation access is not subscription access.
- Tron uses confirmed native/TRC20 histories with fingerprints and explicit gaps; Solana uses finalized signature/transaction RPC with supported parsed transfers and transaction-local ownership. Unsupported instructions/history are not converted into zero activity.
- Reorganisation consistency checks exist in specific decoders, but pinned-head, end-to-end canonical reorg reconciliation is not complete for the six networks.

Explicit query execution is a separate workflow from planning:

1. Prepare `/api/analyses/{job_id}/query-plan` with a 1–200 request budget. The plan records policy, parent job ID, snapshot/result hashes, selected estimates, all deferred/manual/unavailable rows and availability basis.
2. Review selected scopes in the UI. Execute using the plan record's version and SHA-256 plus an idempotency key. Require investigator/admin, current case access, open case, current supporting evidence, supported plan policy, exact parent/specification and configured selected providers in nonfixture mode.
3. One plan can start one job. Repeating its same bound idempotency key returns that job; different input or another spend against a consumed plan is rejected. A further acquisition needs a new plan.
4. Durable `query_checkpoint` records reserve HTTP slots transactionally **before** dispatch. Every reservation/cache mutation is fenced to the active job attempt and current case/actor permissions. Canceled jobs, stale attempts, closed cases and revoked access cannot regain spend authority through that checkpoint.
5. A process loss may consume a reserved slot without an HTTP request or retained response. Never reset that budget on recovery or call a lost response successful. Recovered cached bodies are integrity-checked before parsing; slots/caches span scopes/recovery within this job, not other jobs or tenants.
6. Execute only selected history actions, not manual reviews or an unbounded recursive expansion. New known custody/mixer boundaries are skipped. Preserve partial acquisitions and report outcome/usage per scope.
7. Merge identical protocol observations' provenance. Conflicting event IDs are excluded and produce snapshot 1.2 persistent reconciliation holds, with conflicting normalized observations preserved as artifacts. Reacquisition alone does not clear a hold; there is no implemented automatic reconciliation-release UI. A partial acquisition does not prove an old observation vanished. A newly complete scope missing old outgoing observations excludes them with explicit partial/canonicality-review limits and preserves the parent snapshot. Conflicting bridge-proof IDs fail closed.
8. Recovered complete scopes are downgraded to partial because cached responses do not establish pinned-head consistency. Preserve reviewed labels/proofs and withdrawal holds when producing the new snapshot.
9. Save a superseding result plus plan/parent/summary/new evidence artifacts. The original analysis remains intact. Report durable reservations separately from estimates and vendor billing. Reassessment of this result strips execution-specific request fields and does not reacquire.

Training execution has one explicitly invented extra transfer: `fixture:unresolved` → `fixture:alpha-deposit`, amount `1000000000000000` native Ethereum base units, at timestamp 1750000070, event `fixture-expanded-deposit`. It makes zero HTTP calls and is never a live-failure fallback. Other training scopes without a defined expansion remain not fetched.

<a id="current-8"></a>

## 8. Cross-chain proof scope, security and institutional workflows

The CCTP verifier covers original V2 messenger/transmitter contracts and native USDC between Ethereum domain 0 and Polygon domain 7. Registry version is `circle-cctp-evm-2026-09-24`. Shared supported addresses: MessageTransmitterV2 `0x81d40f21f12a8f0e3252bccb954d722d4c464b64`, TokenMessengerV2 `0x28b5a0e9c621a5badaa536219b3a228c8168cf5d`; native USDC Ethereum `0xa0b86991c6218b36c1d19d4a2e9eb0ce3606eb48`, Polygon `0x3c499c542cef5e3811e1192ce70d8cc03d5c3359`. Recheck official sources before any registry change; these are pinned implementation constants, not a promise of future code/contract identity.

The full protocol note is embedded below. Critical points: exact source MessageSent bytes; normalize only specified off-chain-populated V2 fields; strict ABI/message bounds; Keccak/secp256k1 signature recovery with canonical low-S and ordered distinct known keys; no invented quorum based on key-list length; successful destination receipt and exact MessageReceived/mint correlation; block/transaction/log identity; no removed/duplicate receipt positions; nonce/body/domain/sender/caller/finality/expiration checks; separate burn/fee/net integers; actual destination mint chronology. Supplied keys alone do not prove Circle identity. Receipt agreement is provider consistency, not a receipts-trie/light-client proof. Current public keys do not solve historical key rotation. Other CCTP versions/contracts, Wormhole, generic swaps and hooks remain unsupported.

Imported proofs validate structurally/cryptographically but start as hypotheses. A different authorised reviewer must approve exact record/version/purpose. Withdrawn protocol messages stay blocked even if reacquired until a new independent approval. A signature or successful decoder never identifies a VASP without separate sourced assertions.

Local access controls:

- Passwords use scrypt with random 16-byte salt, N=16384, r=8, p=1, 32-byte derived key and constant-time comparison. Random bootstrap accounts are created only when enabled and no user exists.
- `atlas_session` is HttpOnly/SameSite with server-side hashed tokens, default eight-hour expiry, optional Secure cookie; CSRF token required for authenticated mutations. Password change revokes the user's sessions. The client session-epoch guard discards stale responses after session changes.
- Login throttling is in-process (15 attempts per source in 15 minutes), not distributed identity protection. Host/origin restrictions, security headers, CSP and body-size/chunked-upload checks exist. These are local controls, not a completed penetration assessment.
- Users have investigator/reviewer/admin roles, tenant and case membership. Admin case access remains within the tenant. Wrong-tenant/nonmember case access returns 404. A different reviewer approves consequential evidence, recipients and request payloads; proposer self-approval is rejected.
- Record changes use versions; jobs use idempotency and attempt fences. Local audit rows are not immutable external audit retention.

Recipient/request/response sequence:

1. Propose a recipient's exact entity, jurisdiction, channel, verification source, expiry, purpose and optional Ed25519 public key. A separate reviewer verifies/approves it. No real contacts are seeded or guessed.
2. Prepare `disclosure`, `preservation` or `freezing_review` for an evidenced candidate, with explicit legal basis/scope. Enforce case, recipient entity/purpose/expiry, current evidence and exact body hash. Do not interpret a request type as legal authority.
3. A separate reviewer approves the payload-bound request. Export is enabled only when current checks pass and explicitly remains `NOT_SENT` / `NOT_CONNECTED`.
4. A response must bind to that request ID/hash and reviewed recipient key; verify canonical bytes, signature, scope, interval and replay protection. The format is this application's own `atlas.service-response.v1`, not an official SAHYOG/VASP standard.
5. Independently reviewed structured attestations promote only that exact wallet/entity/category/role/time interval into a case-scoped assertion. Free-form signed findings do not create ownership labels. One signing key shares a provenance family. Training data stays out of operational assertions.
6. Review withdrawal proposals without deleting history. Flag affected analyses, produce case alerts, block stale report/request/bundle exports and reassess into a new result. Historical analysis/snapshot remains available for audit. Already exported files cannot be recalled magically; no implemented cross-case revocation distribution exists.

Do not send real notices, email or portal messages unless explicitly authorised for that action in the current user session. A historical instruction to build an app is not permission to contact a VASP. Never create a fake official SAHYOG response to satisfy a demo.

<a id="current-9"></a>

## 9. Evidence, persistence, jobs and operational limits

Database tables are `users`, `sessions`, `records`, `jobs`, `audit`. Records store typed JSON payloads with tenant/case IDs and versions; kinds include cases, snapshots, assertions, bridges, recipients, requests, feedback, withdrawals, query plans/checkpoints, watches and alerts. Jobs carry original request, actor/case/tenant, unique tenant+idempotency, input hash, status/stage, result/error, lease, attempts and timestamps. There is no separate implemented graph database schema.

Canonical JSON is UTF-8 with recursively sorted object keys via Python `json.dumps`, compact separators, Unicode unescaped and NaN forbidden. It is not claimed to implement RFC 8785. Large amounts are strings. Artifact names are SHA-256 of bytes; reads verify digest and strict hash syntax. Files are written using a temporary sibling and replacement. Local objects/signing keys are not encrypted production WORM storage.

Worker lifecycle uses queued jobs and running jobs with expired leases, transactional compare-and-set claims, 120-second lease renewals, increasing attempts, maximum three claim attempts, cancellation checks and publication fencing. Result states include `COMPLETED`, `COMPLETED_WITH_GAPS`, `FAILED`, `CANCELLED`. Exceptions fail the job without substituting a fixture. Watch scheduling is local, budgets each run, prevents duplicate/closed-case work and generates in-case change alerts; validated distributed scheduling/fairness/backoff is deferred.

PDFs include mode, scope, candidates, paths/assertions, unresolved frontiers, transfer register, exact amounts, protocol details where present, execution parent/plan/usage where present, limits and hashes/version. Bundles contain canonical snapshot/analysis, raw artifacts where acquired/retained, case metadata, audit excerpt, PDF/CSV, metrics and signed manifest. Do not assume a normalized import also contains original independently obtained provider bytes.

Ed25519 signature verification authenticates the bundle relative to its included public key. The offline/UI verifier checks size/path/member safety, exact manifest membership, hashes, signature and deterministic engine replay without executing bundled code. Trusted signer identity requires an independently obtained fingerprint. A valid bundle is not proof of ownership, provider truth, government endorsement or judicial admissibility. Engine changes require new versions plus preserved legacy replay; an old engine rejects new reconciliation holds rather than ignoring them.

<a id="current-10"></a>

## 10. Demo script, UI and reproducible examples

The complete seven-minute guide is embedded below. Short orientation:

1. Open localhost and sign in as investigator. **Open training case** creates a clearly fictional Ethereum investigation shared with local users for reviewer exercises.
2. Inspect Alpha at one hop, Beta at two hops, the mixer branch and the intentionally incomplete `fixture:unresolved` history. Alpha's internal sweep exists in the snapshot but is not traversed through a custody boundary.
3. Inspect the nearestness certificate. Before expansion the missing one-hop branch prevents a stronger bounded claim. The real-world claim remains false regardless.
4. Challenge `fixture-directory` on **Assumptions**. Alpha loses its supporting identity; newly open history may require expansion. Original evidence remains unchanged.
5. On Overview, plan a budget of four requests, inspect selected scope, click **Execute training expansion** then **Start selected queries**. An invented additional Alpha path appears at two hops, a new snapshot is saved and zero provider calls are reported.
6. Export PDF/ZIP; verify the bundle in **Verify evidence**. Explain exactly what integrity/replay proves.
7. Use a clearly training-only recipient for Example Exchange Alpha, independent reviewer approval, payload-bound request and NOT_SENT export. Optionally demonstrate signed scoped response promotion, withdrawal notice and reassessment.
8. Optional: **Review a bridge case**, propose synthetic CCTP proof, independently approve, reassess, inspect burn/fee/net mint, challenge/withdraw. All receipts, service labels and test signing identity are synthetic.
9. Show **Data & coverage** and this requirements matrix. Never hide unavailable API credentials behind the training button.

Training custody seed is `fixture:suspect`, Ethereum; timestamps 1750000000–1750001000. Synthetic events are suspect→Alpha 4 native units, suspect→intermediary 6, intermediary→Beta 5, intermediary→mixer 1, suspect→unresolved 0.001, Alpha deposit→Alpha hot 4. These are invented scenario amounts, not a balanced accounting proof or real intelligence. The fixture uses exact base-unit strings and synthetic evidence IDs.

The UI has case list/search/status filtering/pagination/create/manage, a public and authenticated How it works guide, live source/retrieval-time display and refresh, an evidence side drawer, keyboard/modal support, analysis mode selection and job status, Overview/Evidence/Assumptions/Requests/Monitoring/Audit flows, transfer/wallet inspection, directory review, account/admin actions, coverage screen and independent verifier. Graph has path/circular layout, zoom/fit and resize handling. A mobile min-content overflow in Overview was corrected and verified. A responsive layout does not establish full screen-reader/accessibility certification.

## 14. Troubleshooting and continuation rules

| Symptom | Check first | Correct response |
|---|---|---|
| Cannot sign in | Correct local account, bootstrap file, changed password/session, throttle | Read only the needed local credential when authorised; never reset the database to regain a demo login |
| Page stale/new action absent | Current backend process and `frontend/dist` build | Build/restart only the relevant known local app; preserve existing case data |
| Live result unavailable | Provider configured, credentials/entitlement, timeout and coverage reason | Keep unavailable/partial state; never substitute fixture evidence or hard-code a real exchange |
| No candidate | Labels, validity, finality, chronology, asset, gaps and bounds | Explain unresolved evidence; unknown is a valid result |
| Known nearest true / overall complete false | Depth of remaining frontier vs candidate | Explain bounded nearestness separately from whole graph completeness and real-world identity |
| Budget used but few responses | Durable slots, recovery attempt, timeouts, cached response metadata | Lost reservations still count; do not reset the cap |
| Event vanished after requery | Conflict hold vs complete-scope absence vs failed/pending/finality filter | Inspect parent/received evidence; do not pick a convenient version or erase the hold |
| Bridge proof rejected | Registered version/contracts, exact bytes, nonce/body, key/signature, receipts/log positions, mint, time/finality | Keep unresolved; no amount-only matching or invented key threshold |
| Reviewer cannot execute query | Role boundary | Investigator/admin executes acquisition; do not broaden reviewer privilege to make a test pass |
| Report/request export blocked | Supporting withdrawal, recipient expiry/purpose, payload hash/version | Reassess/review properly; do not bypass stale evidence guard |
| Bundle signature valid but identity untrusted | Independent fingerprint | Report integrity separately; do not call the embedded key government-verified |
| Old report disagrees with code | Report edition and source fingerprints | Historical report is future architecture; current implementation/status controls |
| A test fails after a change | Relevant isolated reproduction and actual exception | Fix the cause; do not delete/relax the meaningful assertion or mark a failed run successful |

Keep progress messages short and factual. The user values working results, distinctiveness, honest claims and avoiding unnecessary delays/permission questions. They designated the assistant as the technical builder; no team size or deadline was supplied. Do not invent an SIH deadline, budget, cloud account or team. Do not install unrelated plugins or send external communications because a reference mentions them.

## 16. Complete final solution, product identity and PPT-ready narrative

Edition: 25 September 2026. This is the consolidated **intended final product**, followed by its current implementation boundary. The complete original 41-section research report remains embedded in the single project skill; this document does not replace or shorten that report. Do not describe the proposed deployment below as already running.

## Identity and central promise

**Name:** TraceSetu. **Sanskrit-derived styling:** Trace + Setu / सेतु. **Pronunciation guide:** Trace SAY-too. **Tagline:** Follow the funds. Find the receiving service.

The user selected **TraceSetu** on 25 September 2026: English *trace* plus Sanskrit *setu* (bridge). It represents the bridge from a transaction trail to supported investigative action. No trademark availability or global uniqueness is claimed. Former names were Vittanvaya and Custody Atlas. The earlier etymology used [vitta](https://www.sanskrit-lexicon.uni-koeln.de/scans/MWScan/MWScanpdf/mw0965-vitta.pdf) and [anvaya](https://www.sanskrit-lexicon.uni-koeln.de/scans/MW72Scan/MW72Scanpdf/pg_0047.pdf); those links are historical naming research, not the derivation of TraceSetu. VittaSutra was rejected after finding [an existing project](https://devpost.com/software/vittasutra). Current app, deck and deliverables use TraceSetu; signed historical evidence, internal schemas, ATLAS_ configuration keys and the stable custody-atlas-sih skill identifier preserve compatibility.

**One sentence:** TraceSetu turns an unknown wallet into a bounded, evidence-backed map of the first reachable custodial services, explains what could change that answer, and prepares the correct reviewed next action.

**Beneficiaries:** investigators and supervisors handling cybercrime cases; technical analysts who need reproducibility; agency administrators managing data access; legal/request teams selecting the correct entity and channel. The user supplied MHA / I4C, CIS Division, SIH Software / Blockchain & Cybersecurity context. No official affiliation, approval, problem ID or exclusive government deployment is asserted.

## The complete end-to-end product we intend to deliver

1. **Authorised intake.** SAHYOG, another authorised case system or the standalone investigator workspace submits a case reference, chain-qualified wallet, relevant time window, optional incident transaction, investigation purpose and query budget. Identity, role and case access are checked before work begins. Address validation and seed semantics prevent mixing an address-wide neighbourhood search with tracing a specific incident payment.
2. **Capability and cost preflight.** The engine snapshots the installed provider capabilities, licensed label scope, finality policy and available budget. It states what can be fetched on this chain, which token/history/trace endpoints are supported, and which attribution sources are missing. Unknown coverage remains visible.
3. **Evidence acquisition.** Durable workers query permitted public or contracted chain APIs, archive responses with source/time/hash metadata, reconcile pagination and receipts and checkpoint work. Shared public-chain evidence may be cached; private case annotations never enter another tenant's case. Provider errors, quota limits and partial history appear in the output.
4. **Canonical multichain normalisation.** Convert observations into chain-qualified events without rounding native units. Preserve Bitcoin outpoints, EVM transaction/log/trace positions, Tron event identity, and Solana instruction/account semantics. Exclude failed or non-final events from confirmed conclusions. Reorganisations append corrected observations and invalidate affected conclusions; they do not silently rewrite old reports.
5. **Attribution resolution.** Keep independent assertions for address ownership, deposit/hot-wallet role, cluster membership and service/legal entity mapping. Each assertion carries provenance, scope, validity, licensing and review state. A sweep pattern can suggest a cluster but cannot establish ownership by itself. Conflicts remain reviewable; an unknown label is not a self-custody classification.
6. **First-custody frontier.** Search each branch in causal transaction order within explicit depth, time, graph-size and request limits. Stop each branch at its first supported custodial service. Do not follow an exchange's later withdrawals as if they were the same customer's funds. Preserve equal-depth alternatives, unresolved branches and the scope of the nearestness claim. An exchange, custodian, mixer, bridge or swap service is classified according to evidence, not a name guessed from activity.
7. **Supported cross-chain continuation.** Use protocol-specific proof verification to relate source and destination events. Preserve bridge fees, asset mappings and source/destination event order. A matching amount and time is a hypothesis, not a certified link. Stop at unknown bridges, mixers or ambiguous swap services and record the missing proof needed to continue. Add protocols through independent certification suites, not a universal bridge checkbox.
8. **Amount and risk interpretation.** Display observed transfer amounts separately from conservative incident-attributable bounds. Never sum overlapping path upper bounds into a recovered balance. Keep attribution strength, history completeness, fund continuity and risk reasons distinct. Explainable indicators may flag obfuscation, rapid dispersal or exposure to a sourced high-risk entity. An indicator is an investigative lead, not a finding of guilt. Calibrated probability scores require a suitable independent labelled corpus; until then use explicit evidence grades and abstention.
9. **Decision-oriented workspace.** Show the custody frontier, graph, supporting paths, evidence drawer, coverage gaps and request destination. The investigator can challenge a source or assumption and see which conclusions disappear. The planner ranks the next permitted evidence requests under a budget, protects unresolved nearer branches and records why a query was selected. Execute only the reviewed selected scopes in a new version of the analysis.
10. **Evidence and reporting.** Generate a human-readable report, structured case export, transfer table and signed manifest of raw artifacts, snapshot and analysis versions. A separate verifier checks file integrity and deterministic replay. A signature proves integrity relative to the signer; it does not prove a label is true, the signer is an exchange or a court will admit the report.
11. **Correctly routed, reviewed action.** Resolve the evidenced service to an agency-maintained legal entity and verified delivery channel. Build a minimal disclosure or asset-preservation/freezing request package with legal basis, scope, hashes and immutable recipient version. A separate authorised reviewer approves the exact payload. The official integration adapter sends only when credentials, institutional approval and API contracts exist. Record acknowledgements, retries and service response status. Blockchain analysis itself cannot freeze funds.
12. **Governed feedback.** Authenticated, request-bound service responses may support narrowly scoped new assertions after independent review. Preserve signature/key provenance, case and time restrictions, contradictory responses and withdrawals. A valid digital signature without a trusted identity binding is insufficient. Corrections create new analysis versions and invalidate stale requests where necessary.
13. **Monitoring and alerts.** Authorised watches rescan defined subjects from durable checkpoints, deduplicate alerts and indicate why a newly observed custody event or risk signal matters. The full product will support reliable scheduling, delivery policies and operational escalation. It will not silently imply continuous monitoring after a local process has stopped.
14. **Operational closure.** The case records actions taken, service receipts, limitations and reviewer decisions. Retention/deletion respects legal holds and agency policy. Administrators observe provider failures, queue health, budget use, restore readiness and access-control violations without putting sensitive case contents into logs.

## Complete system architecture and technology choices

```text
Authorised SAHYOG / external intake             Investigator + reviewer dashboard
                 \                              /
                Authenticated API, tenant/case policy, validated scope
                                      |
                       Case / job / evidence orchestration
                                      |
                 Budgeted acquisition and attribution workers
                 /                    |                    \
       Chain history APIs      Licensed intelligence     Reviewed agency labels
                 \                    |                    /
                Canonical events + versioned assertions + coverage
                                      |
          Temporal custody-frontier search + certified bridge decoders
                                      |
       Candidates / limits / source challenge / next-query recommendations
                         /                          \
          Reproducible signed reports        Request drafting and review
                                                      |
                                      Official integration outbox / receipts
                                                      |
                                      Verified VASP / SAHYOG destination
```

**Current executable profile:** React, TypeScript, Vite, Cytoscape, Lucide; Python, FastAPI, Pydantic and SQLAlchemy; SQLite WAL; local content-addressed evidence; in-process durable-job runner with persisted state; ReportLab and Ed25519-signed ZIP evidence. The skill contains exact inspected versions and contracts. This is a functioning independent local application, not a hosted agency production environment.

**Full deployment profile, proposed:** retain the React/FastAPI domain layer; move authoritative records to PostgreSQL with validated migrations and tenant isolation; use Temporal for durable asynchronous activities; store evidence in encrypted S3-compatible storage with validated retention and legal-hold support; use a rebuildable Neo4j graph projection if benchmarking justifies it. A transactional outbox keeps projection and dispatch consistent with authoritative state. Add agency OIDC/SSO, MFA, workload identities, managed secrets and independent signing-key custody. Instrument using OpenTelemetry, Prometheus/Grafana and redacted structured logs. Containerise deployment; validate backup restoration, failover, patching and alert ownership. Kafka/ClickHouse are optional scale-driven additions, not dependencies already present.

**Separation of trust:** chain providers provide observations; intelligence vendors provide assertions; the agency directory supplies reviewed legal-entity/channel mappings; reviewers authorise consequential action. Acquisition workers do not hold dispatch credentials. An LLM is not the source of transaction facts or ownership claims. An optional drafting assistant could explain already authorised evidence in future, with references and human review.

**Authoritative data:** tenant/user/grant, case/subject, run/checkpoint/budget, block/transaction/event, UTXO/spend, source artifact, assertion/version, cluster membership, bridge link, candidate/path, coverage, plan/execution, report/manifest, recipient/version, request/approval/receipt, watch/alert, audit. Record valid time separately from retrieval time. Native amounts travel as integer/decimal strings. A derived graph can be rebuilt; original evidence cannot be reconstructed merely from a graph screenshot.

**Scaling behaviour:** limit work per case, paginate and checkpoint acquisitions, cache permitted public observations, share immutable artifacts by content hash, partition large event stores, apply per-provider quotas and fair scheduling across tenants, and separate heavy report jobs from intake. Measure throughput before increasing infrastructure. No current benchmark establishes high-volume production readiness.

## Six-chain and service coverage: intended versus built

| Area | Full product commitment | Verified local MVP boundary |
|---|---|---|
| Bitcoin | Certified UTXO history, spend and attribution coverage; explicit CoinJoin/change uncertainty | Read-only Esplora adapter and parser/engine tests; one bounded live Bitcoin sample, browser refresh, raw hash and signed replay verified. Full-chain/ownership certification outstanding |
| Ethereum / BNB Chain / Polygon | Independently certified account, token, receipt and supported internal-trace coverage on each network | Etherscan-family read-only adapters and reconciliation tests; no claimed complete live history |
| Tron | Certified native/TRC20 event history, finality and pagination | TronGrid adapter and tests; live acquisition unvalidated |
| Solana | Certified signature/instruction/token-account ownership interpretation and supported program decoders | RPC adapter and parser tests; incomplete/unsupported instruction cases remain explicit |
| Other chains | Add adapter, capability manifest, decoder/finality tests and evaluation before claiming support | Not presently certified or promised as universal coverage |
| Exchanges / custodians / deposit and hot wallets | Licensed or independently confirmed assertions and reversible entity/role/cluster management | Assertion types and governed review; examples are synthetic; no broad ownership database |
| Mixers / tumblers | Sourced service detection and clear uncertainty stop; no promised deanonymisation | Tagged stops and ambiguity handling; no universal detector |
| DeFi bridges / cross-chain swaps | Protocol-certified event linkage with fee and asset accounting; unresolved boundary otherwise | CCTP V2 USDC Ethereum–Polygon proof pipeline exercised synthetically; not general swap tracing |
| High-risk ecosystems / typologies | Licensed/official source ingestion, explainable rules, triaged alerts and independent quality checks | Risk/coverage presentation and local watches; no broad ransomware/darknet/terrorism intelligence feed |
| SAHYOG / VASP action | Official authenticated intake, reviewed routing, dispatch, receipts and escalation | Standalone case API and reviewed exports; NOT_CONNECTED / NOT_SENT |

## What differentiates the solution

The defensible positioning is **decision-grade custody attribution under uncertainty and budget**, demonstrated through five connected mechanisms. It is not a claim that no existing vendor has comparable functionality.

1. **Bounded custody frontier with an inspectable nearestness certificate.** The output tells an investigator which first custodial boundaries are supported and where incomplete nearer branches could change the answer. Acceptance: preserve equal-depth candidates; stop at the first custody boundary; a missing nearer branch prevents a global-nearest claim.
2. **Challenge the evidence, not just view a graph.** Disable a supporting source as a counterfactual and show which paths survive. Acceptance: removing the sole assertion removes its candidate; independent surviving evidence remains visible; the original analysis is retained.
3. **Spend the next query where it can resolve uncertainty.** Use request budgets, endpoint costs, cache and durable reservations to choose and execute selected evidence queries. Acceptance: no duplicate execution, no overspend across retries, no undisclosed fallback, preserve parent evidence. The training expansion deliberately uses zero provider HTTP calls; it is not a measured live API savings claim.
4. **Evidence that another reviewer can replay.** Export raw references, snapshot, result, versioned contracts and signed manifest; verify integrity and deterministic replay separately from factual truth. Acceptance: tampering fails verification and the unchanged bundle reproduces the supported result.
5. **Reviewed feedback improves future work without erasing provenance.** Request-bound service confirmation can become an assertion only after identity, signature, scope and review checks. Acceptance: wrong signer/scope/request, conflicting evidence or a withdrawn assertion cannot silently establish ownership.

**Why this may be efficient:** bounded search avoids unrestricted graph expansion; stopping at custody avoids meaningless exchange-ledger traversal; cache prevents repeat permitted acquisitions; budget selection prioritises unresolved nearer branches; immutable reuse avoids repeating the entire investigation after each addition. These are implemented or proposed mechanisms, not quantified superiority. Evaluate against depth-first/manual/breadth-first baselines on the same permitted corpus; report accuracy with coverage, abstention, cost and investigator time.

**Practical cost-saving choices:** start independently of SAHYOG access; use allowed public chain APIs and verified user imports; reserve paid labels for the unknown assertions they actually resolve; test deterministic edge cases offline; use open-source software where its licence and operational features fit. Do not bypass API restrictions or relabel synthetic data as a clever live workaround.

## Explicit outputs for an investigator

An answer should include case/run IDs, seed and time range, acquisition limits, provider/source status, candidate entity and wallet role, receiving chain/address, chronological supporting transfers, first-custody hop position, relevant bridge proof, observed amounts and separate attribution bounds, source conflicts, coverage gaps, risk reasons, nearestness scope, next suggested queries, reviewer notes and export hashes. If evidence is missing, return a useful unresolved result with the missing source or query, not an exchange guessed to make the demo look complete.

Example: an invented suspect sends to an intermediary, which sends to a synthetically labelled deposit address. The system shows Example Exchange Alpha at the first supported custody boundary. Challenging its sole source makes that attribution unresolved. A separate unresolved branch can justify another budgeted query. These fixture results demonstrate the workflow, not ownership of a real exchange address.

## Validation and complete-product release gates

The local MVP baseline has 107 passing backend tests and four passing browser workflows, recorded before the branding/video change. Rebranding is a presentation change; record its own build/browser/report checks. These counts establish tested cases, not general-world accuracy. The twelve local MVP checklist checks and the 95-task full-product backlog have different scopes.

Before calling the full product operational, complete: credentialed six-chain end-to-end checks; licensed entity coverage and redistribution rights; official SAHYOG schema/auth/sandbox/access; reviewed jurisdiction-specific request policy and recipient directory; independent ground truth and held-out evaluation; calibrated confidence only if justified; security assessment and agency identity integration; PostgreSQL migrations and tenant-isolation tests; realistic load/latency/cost tests; restore/failover/retention/reorganisation drills; trained operational ownership and incident response. Avoid invented completion dates, staffing commitments, monetary estimates or accuracy/recovery percentages. The original report contains a historical indicative 24–32-week plan, not a current delivery promise.

## PPT instructions for another AI

Use this section and the current-state sections of SKILL.md to explain the solution. Use the embedded original report for detailed design and citations. Do not turn the historical future-tense architecture into a description of the current demo. Do not invent team members, college, problem ID, government logos, partnerships, market size, customer adoption, benchmark charts or live API results. If the SIH template is provided later, obey its slide count and required fields instead of this optional outline.

**Narrative:** unknown wallet → delay finding the responsible custodian → evidence-backed custody frontier → show limits and challenge → query within a budget → reviewed correct destination → reproducible evidence → scalable proposed integration. The unique emphasis is deciding what an investigator can responsibly do next.

| Suggested slide | Concrete content and visual | Claim status |
|---|---|---|
| 1. Problem and solution | TraceSetu, tagline, exact PS title, user-supplied MHA/I4C context; wallet-to-custody line | Problem supplied; product independent |
| 2. The operational gap | Address ≠ owner ≠ VASP entity ≠ authorised freeze; multi-hop delay | Research-backed reasoning, no invented statistics |
| 3. Complete proposed workflow | The 14-step workflow condensed into intake → evidence → frontier → review → action → feedback | Target design; label integration proposed |
| 4. Differentiation | Five mechanisms above with one concrete acceptance test each | Mechanisms built locally; superiority unmeasured |
| 5. Working MVP | Actual renamed app screenshot/clip: graph, source challenge, query execution, replay | Synthetic training demonstration, visibly labelled |
| 6. Architecture and chain coverage | Actual stack next to proposed production profile; six-chain capability table | Distinguish adapter implementation from live certification |
| 7. Evidence and safeguards | Raw artifacts → manifest → replay; independent review; no false freeze claim | Tested local controls, external identity/access outstanding |
| 8. Feasibility and validation | 107-test baseline; browser workflows; dependency gates; cost-control mechanisms | Test evidence only; no accuracy percentage |
| 9. Impact and next steps | Measure time to supported custodian, correct recipient rate, abstention, provider spend; requested access/evaluation | Intended benefits and measurement plan, not measured outcomes |

**Visual language:** deep navy #132733, teal #087e70, warm amber #E8B469, light neutral #F5F7F9. Use one simple flow per slide; clear source/assumption/unknown badges; actual application screenshots without private credentials. Reserve amber for incomplete coverage and synthetic/proposed caveats. Avoid decorative circuit imagery that takes space from the actual workflow.

**Speaker answers:**

- “Does it identify every wallet?” No. It returns supported candidates or an explicit unresolved result within stated coverage.
- “Can you freeze assets?” The system prepares and routes reviewed requests when official integration exists; the competent service/authority acts. The demo sends nothing.
- “Why not just buy a tracing tool?” This project explores an evidence-first, provider-neutral custody-to-action workflow with source challenge, query budgets and reproducible review; procurement and comparative evaluation remain necessary.
- “Is it connected to SAHYOG?” Not this installation. The connector contract and credentials are an external gate; no official API was invented.
- “Is six-chain support live?” Six read-only adapters are implemented and tested with controlled responses. A bounded public Bitcoin sample and browser refresh worked with real data. Other wallet adapters and broader completeness/attribution certification remain outstanding.
- “What is genuinely working now?” Local authenticated cases, bounded analysis, graph/evidence review, source challenge, budgeted training expansion, governed CCTP proof exercise, signed report bundles/replay and reviewed request/feedback controls.
- “Why no percentage confidence?” Calibrated probabilities require independent ground truth. Honest evidence grades are preferable to unvalidated certainty.

## Text equivalents of the two original research-report figures

The embedded historical report preserves its original `[DIAGRAM:trace]` and `[DIAGRAM:architecture]` insertion markers. These are the complete information content of those vector figures, recorded from `research/build_report.py`, so another AI does not need the PDF to recover the diagrams.

**Historical synthetic token example (gas excluded):** wallet S is the incident seed. S → wallet A (intermediary): 6,000 token units; A → Exchange Beta deposit D2: 5,800 units, two hops from S. S → Exchange Alpha deposit D1: 3,000 units, one hop. S → Service U: 500 units on a dashed unresolved branch. S retains 500; A retains 200. No customer identity is inferred. This 10,000-unit research example is distinct from the current app's native-asset and CCTP fixtures; never describe it as a real transaction or substitute its amounts into the recorded demo.

**Historical proposed architecture figure:** Investigator workspace (case / graph / evidence) → Gateway + case API (identity / permissions) → SAHYOG boundary (approved requests only). Gateway → Durable workflows + workers (fetch / decode / attribute / trace). Workers → Provider gateway (quotas / scoped egress) → External sources (chain facts / intelligence). Workers → PostgreSQL (authoritative records). Workers → Evidence objects (raw bytes / signed manifests). PostgreSQL → Neo4j projection (rebuildable case graph). Figure notes: private case data stays inside the application trust boundary; provider assertions are evidence inputs; dispatch requires separate approval. All infrastructure in this figure is the proposed full deployment, not the current SQLite installation.

## Handover rule

Keep the complete target design, current MVP and external gates together. Preserve all exact requirements and source links in SKILL.md. The video is a concise demonstration and cannot replace the complete specification. A new model must inspect current code before changing it, keep the existing local data and historical evidence compatible, and update the skill/checklists after verified changes.
