# Differentiation specification: from an exchange guess to a reviewable action

Updated 25 September 2026 (India time). This is a product/design decision record, not a claim of worldwide uniqueness. The status table distinguishes implemented mechanisms from proposed extensions. The user has deferred the full production build in favor of a working SIH MVP; the 50-page main report remains the future target architecture.

## Product position

For an investigator who starts with an unknown wallet, deliver a ranked first-custody result, an inspectable explanation of the evidence and missing information, the cheapest useful next investigation step, and a reviewed request packet to the verified service entity. The proposed advantage is the combination of decision quality, cost control and reproducibility in the MHA/I4C workflow.

The operational objective is time to a **correct, review-ready receiving-service determination**, subject to an acceptable false-routing rate and known data coverage. Do not optimise for the percentage of queries that return a confident-looking exchange name.

## What the competition already has

| Offering reviewed | Publicly described capability | Consequence for our positioning |
| --- | --- | --- |
| [Chainalysis Reactor](https://www.chainalysis.com/product/reactor/) and [Rapid](https://www.chainalysis.com/product/rapid/) | Entity tracing, graph analysis, triage and packaging intelligence for investigative/legal workflows. | A graph plus an AI summary plus a PDF is not a defensible innovation claim. |
| [TRM Triage](https://www.trmlabs.com/blockchain-intelligence-platform/triage) | Frontline crypto-artifact triage and actionable investigative guidance. | Ease of use and triage alone are insufficient differentiation. |
| [Elliptic Investigator](https://www.elliptic.co/products/investigator/) | Automatic graph generation, entity attribution and one-click cross-chain tracing. | Multi-chain tracing is baseline functionality, not our unique selling point. |
| [GraphSense](https://graphsense.org/) | Open-source analytics, algorithmic transparency and data sovereignty. | Open source, self-hosting and explainability are not unique by themselves. |
| [Iknaio platform](https://iknaio.com/platform/) | Pathfinder, CaseConnect and QuickLock are described as investigation, collaboration and action tooling. | Case linking and action assistance also have prior examples. |

These are supplier descriptions, not hands-on comparisons. Absence of a feature from a public page does not establish that a competitor lacks it. No exhaustive patent, procurement or product evaluation has been performed.

## D1. Bounded first-custody frontier with a nearestness certificate

**Investigator problem:** a platform may return a distant famous exchange while a closer receiving service, a branch, or a missing page is overlooked.

**Implementation:** build a time-respecting frontier that stops each path at its first sufficiently supported custody boundary. Return all qualifying minimum-distance candidates, alternate later paths, unresolved branches and the evidence snapshot. Keep the nearest view separate from an operational-priority view.

**Certificate fields:** seed and scope, ledger checkpoints, normalised movement-event hashes, attribution assertion versions, permissible hop definition, exhausted depths, pruning rules, pagination/decoder gaps, candidate paths and the deterministic algorithm/configuration version.

**Two different claims:** `nearest_known_target_in_snapshot_proven` concerns a deterministic search over the collected, qualified graph. `nearest_real_vasp_proven` concerns reality outside that graph and normally remains false when intermediary custody is unknown. Complete pagination does not imply complete identity labels. An unlabeled intermediary can still be a VASP.

**Acceptance:** an independent offline verifier reproduces the shortest qualified targets in the snapshot; a truncated lower-depth frontier invalidates the corresponding completeness claim; a known closer service cannot be bypassed to reach a famous farther exchange. Adding a newly verified intermediate service correctly changes the first boundary.

## D2. Challenge the answer before an investigator acts

**Investigator problem:** an apparently strong result may depend entirely on one stale label, one inferred cluster or one ambiguous bridge edge.

**Implementation:** run controlled sensitivity checks over the frozen evidence: remove each critical assertion/provenance family, reject heuristic-only edges, change a disputed cluster membership and compare candidates, distances, route validity and amount bounds. Display the smallest known evidence dependencies that make the recommendation change.

**Important limit:** this is evidence sensitivity, not a probability of truth. If removing an assertion opens a previously stopped service frontier for which history was never fetched, return `requires_expansion`; do not pretend the alternative search was complete. Shared upstream label sources are removed together when their dependence is known.

**Acceptance:** a candidate supported only by one assertion loses its supported identity when that assertion is removed. Independent corroboration is visible. The interface explains what changed without inventing a new owner. A disputed mixer link never becomes a verified path solely because it preserves the preferred answer.

## D3. Spend API credits where they can change the decision

**Investigator problem:** blind expansion burns a large query budget on high-fan-out wallets without resolving the actionable receiving service.

**Implementation:** queue unresolved subjects and assign a transparent priority using lower hop distance, whether the question can change the first-custody answer, stale/conflicting identity evidence, incident relevance, endpoint availability and incremental credit cost. Batch compatible label calls and share permitted public-chain cache entries. Reserve a portion of budget for completing shallow frontiers.

Use a versioned deterministic policy first. Do not call its score an expected-information-gain probability before that claim has been validated. The UI shows the next query and a reason such as "classifying this one-hop intermediary could supersede the current three-hop candidate."

**Acceptance:** benchmark against chronological breadth-first expansion, a fixed-depth baseline and cost-matched alternatives on the same authorised datasets. Compare useful-answer rate, missed first services, precision, spend and latency. Proposed release gate: no material precision degradation, and a statistically reported cost or time advantage on the held-out workload. No efficiency percentage is claimed before measurement.

## D4. Turn authenticated VASP responses into reusable evidence

**Investigator problem:** an agency repeatedly asks the same service to resolve a deposit address, while old responses remain unstructured in case attachments.

**Implementation:** accept the service response as an immutable artifact, validate its authorised channel/signature, capture the precise address/control/role/time scope and require review before publishing a new service assertion. Associate legal-entity and recipient versions. Re-evaluate dependent cases after confirmation or correction, subject to agency permissions and data-use rights.

**Trust rule:** a signature proves control of a key; it does not identify a VASP unless that key's binding was independently verified. A PDF on official-looking letterhead is not automatically authenticated. KYC and customer-account data stay restricted; approved reusable service-level facts are separated from private case data.

**Acceptance:** forged/untrusted responses are quarantined. A valid scoped confirmation upgrades only its permitted subject/interval. A correction retracts affected findings and generates a superseding report. Cross-agency information cannot leak through the cache or feedback channel.

## D5. A portable evidence package that survives provider changes

**Investigator problem:** a graph screenshot is hard to reproduce when a subscription ends or labels change.

**Implementation:** export original artifacts where permitted, normalised facts, a minimal selected graph, assertion versions, assumptions, coverage, software/configuration versions and a signed manifest. Provide an offline integrity and replay verifier. The report distinguishes source authenticity, integrity and attribution truth.

**Acceptance:** replay reproduces material findings; modified files fail hash verification; missing licensed artifacts are disclosed rather than fabricated. The verifier does not certify judicial admissibility or convert a supplier assertion into a fact.

## Practical, lawful engineering shortcuts

1. **Use existing parsers and indexing infrastructure where appropriate.** Evaluate GraphSense components and per-dataset TagPack licences rather than rebuilding every ingestion primitive. Its open-source status does not make every external dataset unrestricted, nor prove six-chain coverage.
2. **Buy intelligence selectively.** Fetch verifiable chain history first, query labels in batches at decision-relevant frontiers, cache only within contract terms and refresh when evidence freshness matters. Measure actual billing units; batching requests may not reduce per-address charges.
3. **Reuse verified service facts, not guessed identities.** Agency-reviewed confirmations and corrected assertions can improve future coverage without exposing customer records.
4. **Make uncertainty productive.** A result can explain the missing dependency and propose an authorised next step instead of manufacturing a service name.
5. **Use provider-specific fallback honestly.** An unavailable API produces a resumable partial result. It never becomes an invisible synthetic response or an unauthorised scraped substitute.

## What we will not pitch as innovation

Generic AI summaries; a decorative blockchain dashboard; unsupported "99% accuracy"; magic mixer deanonymisation; a universal freeze button; identifying beneficial owners from public wallet syntax; simulated SAHYOG receipts presented as live integration; or renamed standard graph algorithms.

## Demonstration designed to reveal failures

Use visibly labelled synthetic cases for adversarial algorithm demonstrations and separate independently reviewed real-chain cases for factual evidence validation. Show: a closer VASP hidden by a stale label; a two-source conflict; a completed versus truncated shallow frontier; an ambiguous bridge; a mixed-funds amount bound; a controlled credit budget; an authenticated confirmation and a forged reply; and a tampered evidence file.

The demonstration should allow a reviewer to change an input or remove an assertion and observe the result change. A fixed video or handpicked graph alone does not pass.

## Status and next engineering gates

| Capability | Current status | Proof needed |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Competitive differentiation specification | Researched and recorded | Continue reviewing new evidence; never claim exhaustive uniqueness. |
| First-custody certificate | Implemented; synthetic regression and replay tests pass | Independent labelled evaluation and real provider coverage. |
| Assumption challenge | Implemented; newly opened frontier and browser checks pass | Wider adversarial and real-world evaluation. |
| Query planner | Cost-aware advisory allocation plus explicit selected-plan execution, immutable parent preservation, durable per-job budgets/cache across recovery and conflict holds; API/browser checks pass | Live execution validation, cross-job quotas, cost-matched held-out benchmark and ablation. No measured efficiency improvement claim yet. |
| Confirmation feedback | Key/request-bound signed import, scoped promotion, reviewed withdrawal, stale-result guards and reassessment implemented; synthetic API tests pass | Real provider trust onboarding, automatic signed correction formats, permissioned cross-case propagation and distributed revocation. |
| Evidence export/replay | PDF/CSV/JSON/raw evidence, Ed25519 ZIP and offline verifier implemented | Licensed artifact policy, trusted agency signing and external immutable retention. |
| Full six-chain operational product | Independent application works on explicit training evidence; adapters implemented; CCTP V2 Ethereum/Polygon protocol proof, review and replay work on synthetic cases | Live data validation, additional bridge/swap protocols, security, scale and official integration gates remain. |

No competition-win guarantee or claim that nobody else implements these mechanisms is made. The defensible claim will be the capability set we can demonstrate and the measured benefit it delivers.

# TraceSetu — working SIH MVP

**Current submission:** TraceSetu. Use `output/submission/TraceSetu_ANANTHA_SIH2026_SUBMISSION.pptx`, `output/submission/TraceSetu_Narration_Script.md` and `output/TraceSetu_COMPLETE_SKILL.md`. The live public WannaCry case and provider evidence are in `output/submission/live-case/`; search cases for **PUBLIC-WANNACRY**. The complete delivery audit is `docs/TRACESETU_SUBMISSION.md`.

**New-user help:** select **How it works** in the sidebar, or use the guide link below the sign-in form. It explains the complete workflow, results and limits, with a training shortcut. See `docs/HOW_IT_WORKS.md`.


**Current UI / recording gate (25 September 2026):** the interface has been redesigned and verified. Real public Bitcoin acquisition, a browser-triggered refresh, response hashing and signed live-bundle replay succeeded; this is a bounded sample, not six-chain or ownership certification. Search the case register for **LIVE-BTC** to open the neutral public-data demonstration. Use **Open training case** for the labelled custody/USP exercise. See [current UI review and 2:40 demo sequence](#record-docs-ui-redesign-and-live-demo-md). The user subsequently authorized the real WannaCry case recording and approved TraceSetu. See `docs/TRACESETU_SUBMISSION.md` for the current deliverables and precise limits.


Formerly **Vittanvaya**, originally **Custody Atlas**. Sanskrit-derived name: **Trace + Setu / सेतु**. Tagline: **Follow the funds. Find the receiving service.** Historical evidence, schema identifiers and `ATLAS_` settings retain compatibility. Full target solution and presentation guidance: `docs/FINAL_SOLUTION_AND_PRESENTATION.md`.

**Current scope:** a local investigation workbench that demonstrates the complete wallet-to-evidence-to-reviewed-request workflow. The user requested an MVP instead of continuing the full production build. Every claim below separates working local behavior from unavailable institutional access and remaining live-data validation. A bounded public Bitcoin sample/refresh is now verified; see the current UI/live-data note below.

Open **http://127.0.0.1:8787/**. Use the local investigator account. The three bootstrap account credentials are in `.local/bootstrap-credentials.txt`; do not include that file in a submission or presentation. A separate reviewer account is used for approvals.

## The USP

**“Find the first evidenced receiving VASP, explain why it is the nearest supported answer, and show exactly what evidence could change that answer.”**

The distinguishing demonstration combines four working capabilities:

1. **A bounded custody frontier:** each branch stops at its first supported custodian. Missing shallow paths, ambiguous order and conflicting labels remain visible. An exchange withdrawal is not treated as a continuation through the exchange's private customer ledger.
2. **An inspectable nearestness certificate:** the answer binds to its snapshot, time window, asset and traversal limits. It distinguishes the nearest known target in that snapshot from the unknown nearest VASP in the real world.
3. **A source challenge:** remove an assertion's provenance family and recompute the answer. Investigators can see whether one weak source is carrying an attribution.
4. **Budgeted next-evidence execution:** select a request budget, inspect the proposed frontier queries and execute them into a new result. The request cap survives worker recovery, parent evidence is preserved, and contradictory event observations remain withheld.

Together these make uncertainty actionable and the evidence reproducible. They are an implemented product proposition, not a claim of world-first invention or measured superiority over commercial services. Investigation-time reduction and cost savings require a comparative user/data evaluation.

## Seven-minute primary demo

| Time | Action | Explain |
|---|---|---|
| 0:00–0:45 | Sign in; choose **Open training case** | The whole case is explicitly invented. No real exchange attribution is claimed. |
| 0:45–1:45 | Inspect the graph, Alpha's one-hop boundary, Beta's two-hop boundary, mixer stop and incomplete branch | The engine seeks the first evidenced custodian and does not hide missing data. Base-unit amounts remain exact. |
| 1:45–2:30 | Open **Assumptions**; challenge the `fixture-directory` source family | Alpha disappears when its supporting source is removed. The original analysis is preserved. |
| 2:30–3:30 | Return to Overview; **Plan within a request budget**, enter **4**, save; **Execute training expansion**, inspect scope, then **Start selected queries** | An invented extra transfer resolves one training branch without an HTTP call. The resulting snapshot is new. In configured live mode the same executor uses real parsers and a hard shared job budget; live validation remains outstanding. |
| 3:30–4:30 | Download the report and signed ZIP from **Evidence**; open **Verify evidence** and verify the ZIP | Integrity and deterministic replay work. Signature validity alone does not establish a provider's identity or the truth of an ownership claim. |
| 4:30–6:30 | Use a synthetic recipient in **VASP directory**, independently approve as reviewer, prepare a disclosure/preservation package, independently approve its payload, export | The request targets the evidenced entity and requires a verified contact. The export explicitly says NOT_SENT / NOT_CONNECTED. No government integration is pretended. |
| 6:30–7:00 | Show **Data & coverage** and the requirements table below | Six adapters, known limits and actual integration dependencies are explicit. |

For a shorter pitch, demonstrate the first four minutes and show a pre-created reviewed request package. The reproducible browser checks create training examples; do not describe them as real investigations.

## Optional cross-chain demonstration

Choose **Review a bridge case**. Its invented CCTP proof initially produces no custody attribution. In **Evidence**, propose the training proof. Approve it from the separate reviewer session, then **Reassess recorded evidence**. Inspect the Ethereum → Polygon path and the distinct burn, fee, net mint and destination arrival order. Challenge or withdraw the proof to remove its supported path. This covers one tightly scoped protocol, not universal cross-chain deanonymisation.

## Every problem-statement requirement, mapped honestly

| Requirement | Working MVP behavior | Boundary / remaining dependency |
|---|---|---|
| Analyse reported suspect wallets through APIs | Authenticated case and analysis APIs, input validation, durable jobs, imports and provider mode | Receiving an actual SAHYOG report requires its official contract and credentials. |
| Nearest exchange/custodial service/direct-deposit receiver | Chronological first-custody traversal, deposit-role assertions, direct and multi-hop candidates, conflict/unknown states | A deposit role requires sourced evidence. Missing identity labels cannot be inferred from transactions alone. |
| Bitcoin, Ethereum, Tron, BNB Chain, Solana, Polygon | Six bounded read-only chain adapters with exact amount normalization and raw-response preservation | Parsers are tested with explicit synthetic inputs. Real endpoints/entitlements and independent per-chain examples still require validation. Other chains are future adapters. |
| Exchange clusters, hot wallets and deposit wallets | Source assertions carry cluster/hot/deposit/unknown roles; reviewers control their promotion | Automated ownership clustering is not implemented. A cluster assertion is not beneficial-owner identification. |
| Mixers/tumblers | Source-labelled mixer boundary stops deterministic traversal | No guessed mapping from mixer deposits to withdrawals. |
| DeFi bridges and cross-chain mapping | CCTP V2 native-USDC Ethereum/Polygon proof correlation, review, graph and report inspection | Other bridges require separate verified decoders and trust analysis. |
| Cross-chain swaps / DeFi | Unsupported asset/protocol transitions are exposed as unresolved; service categories can be represented by assertions | General swap/aggregator decoding and opaque off-chain swap correlation are not implemented. |
| Blockchain intelligence and graph analytics | Provider adapter layer, strict assertion/proof contracts, deterministic graph engine and interactive graph | Commercial attribution data needs licensed API access; a transaction-history API is not an ownership database. |
| Automated tagging and confidence scoring | Source-linked service/risk tags, reviewed/provider/hypothesis grades, evidenced/conflicted/hypothesis result states and a nearestness certificate | Grades are categorical confidence evidence. Calibrated percentage scores are deliberately absent until independent labels support them. |
| LEA investigation reports | PDF, JSON, CSV, preserved raw evidence, signed manifest and offline/UI replay | Admissibility and agency report acceptance are institutional decisions; signatures establish integrity. |
| Route lawful disclosure/freezing requests | Candidate-to-recipient matching, expiry checks, two-person review, payload binding and export; signed response review | Official SAHYOG transport and real sending are not connected. A prepared freezing-review request is not an asset freeze. |
| Case dashboard and real-time intelligence | Interactive case workbench, queued/running/completed/partial jobs, current local result updates | Provider latency and incomplete history prevent a universal real-time guarantee. |
| Risk and high-risk wallet alerts | Sourced risk categories, explicit unassessed state, watched-wallet case change alerts | No invented criminal label, numerical risk calibration or external notification service. Dedicated laundering-typology detection is future work. |
| Scale / large transaction volumes | Bounded request/state/hop limits, durable local worker and configurable database connection; architecture documented | Current validation is single-node/local. No large-volume performance or production PostgreSQL claim. |
| Reduce time, improve freezing efficiency and cross-border investigations | Query prioritisation, reproducible evidence and reviewed recipient/request workflow support these aims | Benefits are hypotheses until measured. Real freezing and cross-border action require legal authority and VASP cooperation. |

## Demonstrable acceptance and practical cost-saving choices

The complete local backend regression passed **107 tests**, including 18 execution tests. The frontend compiles and builds. All four browser workflows passed against the final build: investigator, independent reviewer, protocol-proof review and selected query execution. They reported zero page errors. The query workflow passed mobile overflow checks, preserved its parent and verified its signed bundle; its two-page PDF was rendered and visually inspected. See `MVP_CHECKLIST.md` for acceptance evidence.

The app can be demonstrated without purchasing an intelligence API: use clearly labelled synthetic scenarios or lawfully acquired evidence imports. Optional permitted public/self-hosted sources and later credentialed providers use the same evidence contracts. Persisted responses are reused after execution recovery, saving duplicate HTTP requests where evidence was already preserved. This does not bypass licensing or establish quantified savings.

## Deliberately outside this MVP release

Agency SSO/MFA, production KMS/object retention, cross-tenant scheduling, high-volume benchmarks, deployment security accreditation, calibrated real-world attribution, general bridge/swap support and official SAHYOG dispatch remain in the [future backlog](#record-implementation-checklist-md). The research PDF describes the full target architecture; this document defines the smaller current release. No additional production build is required before presenting this MVP honestly.
<!-- END EMBEDDED SIH_MVP_GUIDE.md -->

## Update rules

1. Implement the narrowly named task, run its meaningful acceptance checks, inspect the result and record the evidence.
2. Only then change its checkbox to checked and update `docs/status.md` when a capability changes.
3. Run `python scripts/update_checklist.py` to refresh counts. This script only counts boxes; it never decides a task is complete.
4. Reopen a checked item if a regression invalidates its evidence. Keep synthetic, local, live and official acceptance distinct.
5. Do not call the production platform complete while applicable production gates remain open. The revised MVP can be handed over when its separate checklist passes. Do not ask for repeated approval for already authorised work.

# TraceSetu

**Current submission:** TraceSetu. Use `output/submission/TraceSetu_ANANTHA_SIH2026_SUBMISSION.pptx`, `output/submission/TraceSetu_Narration_Script.md` and `output/TraceSetu_COMPLETE_SKILL.md`. The live public WannaCry case and provider evidence are in `output/submission/live-case/`; search cases for **PUBLIC-WANNACRY**. The complete delivery audit is `docs/TRACESETU_SUBMISSION.md`.

**New-user help:** select **How it works** in the sidebar, or use the guide link below the sign-in form. It explains the complete workflow, results and limits, with a training shortcut. See `docs/HOW_IT_WORKS.md`.


**Current UI / recording gate (25 September 2026):** the interface has been redesigned and verified. Real public Bitcoin acquisition, a browser-triggered refresh, response hashing and signed live-bundle replay succeeded; this is a bounded sample, not six-chain or ownership certification. Search the case register for **LIVE-BTC** to open the neutral public-data demonstration. Use **Open training case** for the labelled custody/USP exercise. See [current UI review and 2:40 demo sequence](#record-docs-ui-redesign-and-live-demo-md). The user subsequently authorized the real WannaCry case recording and approved TraceSetu. See `docs/TRACESETU_SUBMISSION.md` for the current deliverables and precise limits.


Formerly **Vittanvaya**, originally **Custody Atlas**. Sanskrit-derived name: **Trace + Setu / सेतु**. Tagline: **Follow the funds. Find the receiving service.** Historical evidence, schema identifiers and `ATLAS_` settings retain compatibility. Full target solution and presentation guidance: `docs/FINAL_SOLUTION_AND_PRESENTATION.md`.

An independent blockchain investigation workbench for the SIH VASP attribution problem. The research report is in `output/pdf/`. The product mandate is in `AGENTS.md`.

**Status:** working local SIH MVP. The user has deferred the full production build. It is not an official SAHYOG integration or an operationally validated attribution service. Read `docs/status.md` for implemented, tested and outstanding capabilities.

**Current release:** [MVP checklist](#record-mvp-checklist-md) and [SIH demo guide, USP and requirement mapping](#record-sih-mvp-guide-md). The [full-product checklist](#record-implementation-checklist-md) is preserved as a future backlog, with honest evidence and external gates.

## Run locally

Requirements: Python 3.12, Node.js 22 or newer, pnpm 11.19, and provider credentials for credentialed APIs.

```powershell
python -m venv .venv
.venv\Scripts\python.exe -m pip install -r requirements.lock
cd frontend
pnpm install --frozen-lockfile
pnpm build
cd ..
powershell -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File scripts/start.ps1
```

Open **http://127.0.0.1:8787**. First startup creates three random local account passwords in `.local/bootstrap-credentials.txt`: investigator, reviewer and admin. The application never embeds a universal default password. Keep this file private. Use the account page to change passwords; changes revoke all sessions for that account. Real deployment requires agency identity integration, MFA, transport security, key management and the production gates below.

On Linux/macOS, use `.venv/bin/python`, build the frontend the same way, then `sh scripts/start.sh`. Copy `.env.example` to `.env` only if changing configuration. Never commit credentials, case data or signing keys. The local host allowlist rejects unknown Host headers; update it deliberately for a deployment.

## Exercise the complete independent workflow

1. Sign in as investigator. Choose **Open training case**. The fixture is explicitly synthetic and contains no real service claims. Its case is shared with the local users for review exercises.
2. Inspect Alpha's one-hop boundary and Beta's two-hop boundary, the mixer stop and the pagination gap. Select a graph wallet or transfer to inspect the underlying assertion or event.
3. On **Assumptions**, challenge the `fixture-directory` family. Alpha disappears; the answer becomes dependent on an unfetched branch. The original analysis remains unchanged.
4. Download the report or signed ZIP from **Evidence**. Use **Verify evidence** to check its signature, member hashes and deterministic replay. Changing any signed member must fail verification.
5. In **VASP directory**, choose **Synthetic training recipient** and propose an entry whose entity is exactly `Example Exchange Alpha`, clearly labelled test-only. Sign in as reviewer in a different browser/session and approve it. Training recipients are blocked from operational requests, and operational recipients cannot be used for training requests. No real recipient is preloaded or guessed.
6. As investigator, prepare a disclosure/preservation package from the candidate. The reviewer approves its exact payload. Export is then enabled. The JSON clearly states it has not been sent.
7. In Overview, select **Plan within a request budget** and enter **4**. The plan compares same-depth observed paths per estimated HTTP request, shows provider availability and keeps manual reviews separate. Planning alone fetches nothing. **Execute training expansion** opens a scope review; **Start selected queries** creates a new result with an invented extra transfer and zero HTTP calls. In configured live mode, **Execute selected queries** uses the same hard per-job budget across scopes/recovery and records reserved request slots separately from estimates. Parent evidence is preserved. No measured cost saving is claimed.
8. A response signed by the reviewed recipient key can be imported and independently reviewed. A scoped attestation promotes only the requested wallet/entity/time interval. **Reassess recorded evidence** applies current case assertions to a new result without refetching transfers. A reviewed withdrawal flags affected analyses and blocks stale request/report exports. See `docs/service-feedback.md` for the format and limitations.
9. For a real investigation, create a separate case, explicitly choose members, propose source-backed labels for review, configure providers, and run **Live provider acquisition**. Failed providers remain failed; live analysis never falls back to training data.

For the separate cross-chain exercise, choose **Review a bridge case**. Its CCTP proof starts unreviewed. Propose the training proof in Evidence, approve it from a separate reviewer session, then reassess to reveal the Ethereum-to-Polygon custody path. The graph/report preserve burn, fee, mint, destination time and trust assumptions. Challenge or withdraw the proof to remove the path. See [CCTP protocol scope, imports and trust limits](#record-docs-cctp-md). The optional read-only live adapter is disabled by default and has not been live-validated.

## API and evidence contracts

The OpenAPI schema is available at `/api/openapi.json`. Authentication uses an HttpOnly, SameSite session cookie. All authenticated writes require `X-CSRF-Token` returned by sign-in. Analysis creation also requires a unique `Idempotency-Key` header. Reusing a key with changed input is rejected.

`backend/vasp_app/domain.py` defines Snapshot, Transfer, Assertion, BridgeProof, Coverage and TraceSpec contracts. JSON imports must use the contract exactly; arbitrary extra fields are rejected. Imported labels and bridge proofs begin as hypotheses. Reviewed case evidence can be added to a subsequent analysis without rewriting old evidence. The JSON schemas are in `docs/snapshot.schema.json` and `docs/bridge-proof.schema.json`.

Offline bundle verification:

```powershell
.venv\Scripts\python.exe scripts/verify_evidence.py path\to\evidence.zip
.venv\Scripts\python.exe scripts/verify_evidence.py path\to\evidence.zip --trusted-fingerprint EXPECTED_SHA256
```

The first command checks integrity and replay, **not signer identity**. Obtain the expected public-key fingerprint through a trusted channel. A correct signature does not prove a provider claim, ownership or evidentiary admissibility.

## Verification

```powershell
.venv\Scripts\python.exe -m pytest tests -q
cd frontend
pnpm build
```

The browser checks exercise a running local server and save screenshots/results under `tmp/browser/`. They create clearly labelled synthetic cases and review records, and send no external messages. Playwright is a locked frontend development dependency. Windows uses installed Microsoft Edge; on other systems install Playwright Chromium with `pnpm exec playwright install chromium`. Run from the repository root:

```powershell
node scripts/browser_check.cjs
node scripts/browser_review_check.cjs
node scripts/browser_bridge_check.cjs
node scripts/browser_query_check.cjs
```

The review script expects a training case created by the first script. Bootstrap passwords must remain unchanged for these local test scripts; operational credentials must never be substituted into committed test code.

`Dockerfile`, `compose.yaml` and `.github/workflows/check.yml` are deployment/CI recipes. They have not been run against Docker or GitHub in this environment. Compose binds the application to loopback and requires a URL-safe `ATLAS_DB_PASSWORD` environment value. Bootstrap credentials are written inside its evidence volume. This recipe is for validating an independent installation, not a certified government deployment.

The replay verifier supports the installed engine `0.5.0` and frozen `0.3.0` / `0.4.0` implementations. Old engines reject the new reconciliation-hold extension. Other development versions require their matching engine for computational replay; an unsupported version is reported explicitly.

## Implementation map

- `backend/vasp_app/engine.py`: bounded chronological first-custody paths, certificates, source-family challenge and unresolved-frontier queue.
- `backend/vasp_app/planner.py`: versioned advisory request budgeting with explicit cost assumptions and unavailable-provider states.
- `backend/vasp_app/execution.py`: explicit plan execution, immutable parent binding, durable request reservations/cache and persistent event-conflict holds. Cross-job quotas remain a production backlog item.
- `backend/vasp_app/feedback.py`: narrowly scoped signed service attestations and request/key binding.
- `backend/vasp_app/providers.py`: read-only Bitcoin, EVM (Ethereum/BNB/Polygon), Tron and Solana acquisition with budgets and raw evidence preservation.
- `backend/vasp_app/cctp.py`: CCTP V2 native-USDC message/signature/receipt verification for Ethereum and Polygon, with exact fee/net amounts and destination arrival ordering.
- `backend/vasp_app/main.py`: authenticated case APIs, review workflow, request packages, evidence and signed responses.
- `backend/vasp_app/worker.py`: durable local jobs, claims, leases and case watches.
- `backend/vasp_app/reports.py`: reports, signed bundles and offline verification.
- `frontend/src/`: case workbench, interactive graph, evidence/review screens, monitoring and capability register.
- `research/`: research sources, PDF builder, implementation plan and differentiation requirements.

## Known production gates

Live provider validation and entitlements; licensed identity data; pinned-head/native/internal reconciliation and reviewed asset validation; reorganisation reconciliation; live validation and expansion beyond the implemented Ethereum/Polygon CCTP V2 decoder; independently labelled evaluation; calibrated scoring; scalable workflow/graph/object infrastructure; agency identity/MFA; immutable external audit; key rotation/HSM; backup/restore and performance testing; official SAHYOG API contract, credentials and delivery acknowledgement; deployment security review. These are not hidden behind the training workflow.

The user revised the immediate target on 24 September 2026 to a polished, functioning SIH MVP and explicitly deferred the full production build. Finish and verify the in-progress query execution workflow, preserve the working end-to-end demo, map every problem-statement requirement to its honest capability/limit, and provide a concise demo guide and USP. Do not expand into production infrastructure work now. The original full-product checklist is a future backlog, not the current release gate. ## Mandatory product standards

For the complete self-contained project handover, exact problem statement, current implementation, research/source register, contracts, demo and deferred backlog, use `skills/custody-atlas-sih/SKILL.md`. Read its current-state overview first and retrieve only the relevant deeper sections; the embedded original research report is a historical proposal, not current implementation status. Update the skill when material facts change.

- Differentiation must improve an investigator's outcome and have a reproducible acceptance test. Graphs, multi-chain support, risk scores, case management and legal-request packaging already exist in other products; do not call those alone novel.
- Treat proposed differentiators as hypotheses until implemented and evaluated. Do not claim world-first status, superior accuracy, guaranteed recovery, official MHA endorsement, or production readiness without evidence.
- Never fabricate wallet ownership, exchange labels, transaction paths, confidence percentages, successful API calls, frozen balances or SAHYOG connectivity.
- Distinguish observed chain facts, provider assertions, investigator hypotheses, synthetic fixtures, missing coverage and independently verified service confirmations in both code and UI.
- Live mode must never silently fall back to synthetic data. No label means unknown, not proven self-custody or low risk.
- Identify the first evidenced custodial boundary per path. Do not trace across an exchange's internal customer ledger by linking unrelated withdrawals.
- Preserve exact native amounts, chronology, chain-qualified identities, source evidence and reorganisation handling. Keep amount allocation assumptions separate from actual transfers.
- Every consequential request requires an authenticated user, the appropriate agency/case permission, a verified recipient and payload-bound review. Do not send real notices or messages without explicit session authorisation.
- Public data and permitted self-hosted/open-source components are practical cost-saving options. Do not bypass paywalls, rate limits, authentication or licensing, and do not infer customer identities from deposit addresses.
- Keep a status ledger of implemented, tested, proposed and externally blocked capabilities. A working independent installation does not establish official integration or production-grade attribution.
- Maintain `IMPLEMENTATION_CHECKLIST.md` after each verified milestone, as explicitly requested by the user. Check off only the narrowly stated task after its acceptance evidence passes; preserve remaining scope and external gates. Run `scripts/update_checklist.py` to update counts. Counts are unweighted tasks, never an invented readiness percentage.

## Differentiation priorities

See `research/differentiation.md`. Prioritise the bounded custody frontier, an inspectable nearestness certificate, evidence/assumption challenge, a budget-aware next-query planner, and provenance-preserving feedback from authenticated service responses. Preserve the full six-chain and end-to-end scope in the main report.

## Delivery and verification

Use reproducible fixtures for algorithm tests and independently verified/authorised examples for real-world evaluation. Do not use a vendor's own output as both prediction and independent ground truth. Report accuracy together with answer coverage, abstention, provider cost and investigator time. Update documentation when evidence or implementation changes. Never call an untested connector operational.

# TraceSetu — final submission and live-case handover

Current edition: 25 September 2026. This section supersedes older branding and recording holds. The user chose **TraceSetu** and explicitly requested continuation of the PPT correction, actual public-case test, native 4K film (maximum 2:45), matching narration and comprehensive handover. Recording is authorized. External disclosure messages and freezes are not.

## Identity, purpose and limits

TraceSetu joins English **trace** with Sanskrit **setu**, bridge. Say “Trace SAY-too.” Tagline: **Follow the funds. Find the receiving service.** Former names were Vittanvaya and Custody Atlas. No trademark clearance or global uniqueness is claimed.

The current interface, browser title, API product identity, generated reports, help guide, working documentation and final slide deck use TraceSetu. The stable `custody-atlas-sih` skill path, `ATLAS_` settings, session/schema identifiers and older signed bundles preserve compatibility and provenance. Archived filenames containing former names describe historical artifacts, not the current product. Do not modify signed evidence simply to change branding.

The immediate product is the polished local SIH MVP, not the deferred production platform. End users are cybercrime investigators, separately authorized reviewers and local administrators. It helps an officer inspect the first supported custodial boundary per chronological path and the evidence needed before routing an action. It neither identifies beneficial owners from wallet addresses nor guarantees a nearest real exchange.

The practical differentiator is the combination of **first-custody stopping, an inspectable nearestness certificate, source challenges, a budgeted next-query planner and reviewed service feedback**. These have fixture acceptance tests and clear conditions; no world-first, independent accuracy superiority, proven recovery or measured operational speedup is claimed. Multi-chain graphs, case management and reports alone are not novel.

## Slide decision and changes

Final deck: `output/submission/TraceSetu_ANANTHA_SIH2026_SUBMISSION.pptx`.

The second supplied workflow was clearer, but required corrections. The first included an important independent request-review stage. The final deck combines the clearer layout with that review requirement, removes the redundant workflow and contains six slides. The supplied SIH visual style, team ANANTHA, Team ID 170835 and PS ID 26182 are retained as user-supplied metadata, not official endorsement.

- Slide 1: TraceSetu identity and user-supplied submission details.
- Slide 2: reported wallet → acquisition and evidence → bounded custody analysis → independently reviewed action, export and feedback. Candidate wallets are not automatically called deposit wallets. Source roles and validity intervals matter.
- Slide 3: actual stack, chronological/outpoint tracing, categorical evidence grades and explicit CCTP limits. Public prototype/repository URLs have editable placeholders; no public URLs were supplied and localhost is not a public deployment.
- Slide 4: current MVP, 116 backend tests and explicitly synthetic custody screenshot; future deployment and official-integration gates stay separate.
- Slide 5: public context and intended benefits. The ₹1,646 crore figure is the ED's 15 February 2025 BitConnect release. The 35 VASPs figure is a dated 23 July 2025 MHA parliamentary answer, not a present-day total. TraceSetu has no measured recovery amount. Competitor capabilities are not presented as absent merely to manufacture novelty.
- Slide 6: research sources, current role/limits and the previously missing ED reference.

Actual stack: React, TypeScript, Vite, Cytoscape.js, Lucide; Python, FastAPI, Uvicorn, Pydantic, HTTPX; SQLAlchemy, SQLite WAL and a local worker; local SHA-256-addressed evidence objects; ReportLab and cryptography/Ed25519. No LLM attribution engine, running Neo4j, Temporal, production PostgreSQL cluster or deployed SAHYOG connection is implied. Six adapters exist; Bitcoin has a bounded live sample. Broad live checks of the other chains are pending. CCTP V2 USDC Ethereum–Polygon verification is synthetically tested; live proof validation remains pending.

## Real public case and evidence

Case: **WannaCry ransomware, public historical Bitcoin study**. CERT-In's published alert, page 4, displays `13AM4VW2dhxYgXeQepoHkHSQuy6NgaEb94`. The source establishes why this starting address is relevant; it is not proof of downstream ownership, an active police case, or a verified VASP label.

Source PDF SHA-256: `dc8c96c2dd908fbfa9cf40e561d9c8c598f1c4b1d5639c55a6c6bcb5b47d3389`. A preserved copy is `output/submission/live-case/CERT-In_WannaCry_Alert.pdf`. Page 4 was visually checked. The investigation period is 3 August 2017 UTC, one hop. A live query means data is fetched now about historical transactions, not that the incident is current or that a continuous blockchain stream exists.

Actual successful acquisition returned these two transactions and three outputs:

| Transaction | Output | Receiving address | Exact satoshis | BTC |
|---|---:|---|---:|---:|
| `a028bb2d4c795cb8a8fd2f03285934fba8747fa84296fb7711dcda179b21cc4c` | 0 | `1ARirZgU4q61sSjVK2iB8BEYC5w2B8ZnE9` | 10287428 | 0.10287428 |
| Same transaction | 1 | `1H68h8qsVkMUgY8khcdFpbHV22cCnC74dk` | 956285137 | 9.56285137 |
| `8def6458a46234ab0e040602e7852ff5cf58650f3f1102803b1d4bca4cc293a1` | 0 | `1M1CfXLynR6vqbjwTqSiiLRVDQZEXHHJbb` | 1005800019 | 10.05800019 |

**Zero supported VASP candidates were found.** Coverage and hop limits remain visible. The tool does not manufacture a label, an attribution probability or an apparently successful freeze. Its risk classifier has no imported ransomware assertion in this case; the public-source association appears in the investigator's case notes. Unknown risk remains unassessed. Amounts are observed outputs, not recoverable balances or proven incident allocation.

Evidence location: `output/submission/live-case/`. `verification.json` records the latest checked job, scope and counts. `analysis.json`, `snapshot.json`, `TraceSetu_WannaCry_Report.pdf` and `TraceSetu_WannaCry_Evidence.zip` preserve the reviewed run. The original `run.json` preserves initial setup provenance; a later filmed query can have a different job ID. Never confuse inherited transfers in a child analysis with freshly fetched responses; acquisition metrics state their scope.

## Explicit Bitcoin provider alternative and regression evidence

Public Esplora endpoints experienced timeouts from this installation. A selectable BlockCypher adapter was added in `backend/vasp_app/blockcypher.py`; `ATLAS_BITCOIN_PROVIDER=blockcypher` chooses it explicitly. Default configuration remains Esplora. No paywall, authentication, rate-limit or licensing control was bypassed, and no silent fallback was added.

The adapter uses the documented full-address endpoint, 50-row block-height pagination and `txlimit=1000` so large input sets are not silently truncated. It checks confirmations locally, exact integer satoshis, timestamps, address identity, double-spend status and full input/output lengths. It retains input outpoints and ledger position. Unsupported multi-address scripts create partial coverage, not invented links. Budget/rate/response-size limits, raw hashing, checkpoints and cache accounting use the existing acquisition engine.

A live check found that combining `confirmations=1` with `before` repeatedly returned the first page. Omitting that optional server filter while retaining local confirmation checks advanced the cursor. A regression assertion covers the parameter combination. A stalled cursor still fails closed and retains checkpointed events. If the provider omits its history-completion signal, the adapter retains returned transactions but marks coverage partial; absence of a flag is never evidence of complete history. Fresh queries also encountered public-provider read timeouts; these are recorded as incomplete acquisition. Local timeout was increased to 60 seconds for the demo, not masked as success.

The full backend suite passed **116 tests** (historical 107 plus 9 BlockCypher contract cases), recorded in `tmp/tracesetu-backend-junit.xml`. After the pagination-parameter and missing-completion-signal adjustments, all nine provider tests passed, including the added missing-signal regression. The report/bundle/replay/tamper workflow test also passed after replacing a missing-hop report value with “not established.” TypeScript/Vite production build passed following the rename and the follow-up-query scroll correction. Starting a selected-query execution now returns the viewport to the running-job status, avoiding an off-screen progress panel when the prior result disappears. Tests are correctness checks, not accuracy or real-world ownership benchmarks.

## Recording and presenter's claims

Capture: `scripts/record_live_walkthrough.cjs`; rendering: `scripts/render_live_walkthrough.py`. The final file is `output/submission/TraceSetu_Live_Case_4K.mp4`, with actual 3840×2160 Edge frames, an enlarged cursor, click ripples, smooth click-centred zooms and eight timed chapters totaling 160 seconds. The separate narration is `output/submission/TraceSetu_Narration_Script.md`. A silent file is intentional; the user requested a script to speak alongside it. Delivery completion is recorded only after the final encoded file passes decode, playback and visual verification.

The recording demonstrates real acquisition, observed trace, evidence rows, the no-attribution result, certificate, budgeted follow-up, signed export/replay and the conditions for request preparation. Provider waiting time is condensed; it is not a performance benchmark. The recorded case does not demonstrate a live cross-chain route, actual service response, paid intelligence or an externally sent notice. The deck's synthetic custody example is a separate labelled test, never evidence for this ransomware case.

## Additional verified primary references

- [CERT-In WannaCry alert](https://www.csk.gov.in/documents/WannacryWannaCryptRansomware_CRITICAL_ALERT_CERT-In.pdf): public ransomware source and the seed address on page 4; no downstream VASP attribution.
- [MyCERT WannaCry advisory](https://mycert.org.my/portal/advisories?id=fb834148-452a-44d2-858b-7f5c64611d22&page=84&per-page=10): historical technical incident context.
- [BlockCypher full-address API](https://www.blockcypher.com/dev/bitcoin/#address-full-endpoint): provider pagination and transaction-array contract; not a wallet ownership database.
- [Esplora API](https://github.com/Blockstream/esplora/blob/master/API.md): original Bitcoin adapter contract; network reachability can vary.
- [ED BitConnect release, 15 February 2025](https://enforcementdirectorate.gov.in/sites/default/files/latestnews/Press%20Release-Search-%20Bitconnect-15.02.2025.pdf): supports the dated seizure context in the deck, not TraceSetu's impact.
- [ED annual report 2024–25](https://www.enforcementdirectorate.gov.in/media/5y2bfhhj/annual_report_24-25.pdf): supporting official context.
- [MHA Rajya Sabha Q.399, 23 July 2025](https://www.mha.gov.in/MHA1/Par2017/pdfs/par2025-pdfs/RS23072025/399.pdf): dated SAHYOG onboarding context, not evidence of access for this prototype.
- [Chainalysis Reactor](https://www.chainalysis.com/product/reactor/): public competitor features; no unsupported exclusivity claim.

The original exact problem statement, 30 requirement mappings, full 41-section research report, all original research references, the complete intended production solution and deferred backlog remain in the complete skill. Current external gates still include official SAHYOG access, licensed/verified labels, independent field evaluation, approved service recipients and production security/scale validation.

# TraceSetu — current live-case video delivery

The user explicitly authorized the real-case recording and approved TraceSetu on 25 September 2026. This supersedes the earlier UI-approval hold.

Current artifacts are in `output/submission/`: the revised six-slide PowerPoint, real WannaCry evidence, the native 4K live-case film and synchronized narration. See `docs/TRACESETU_SUBMISSION.md` for the complete technical, source and claim audit. The shareable handover is `output/TraceSetu_COMPLETE_SKILL.md`.

The live-case film is designed as eight chapters: 00:00 public case; 00:12 acquisition; 00:40 observed trace; 01:02 evidence; 01:22 certificate/planning; 01:40 bounded follow-up; 01:58 signed-bundle verification; 02:22 reviewed action and coverage. It totals 2:40 at 3840×2160, below 2:45, and is silent with a separate speaking script. Capture, encode, full-decode, playback and visual verification are recorded in `output/submission/live-capture-manifest.json` and `live-video-verification.json`; completion requires those checks.

Real data is retrieved about historical transactions. Provider waits are edited for length, not benchmarked. No unsupported exchange labels, real notices, asset freezes or official SAHYOG connection are shown. The local signature establishes file integrity, not agency identity or wallet ownership.

Historical earlier-UI film: `output/demo/Vittanvaya_SIH_MVP_4K.mp4`. It is synthetic, 160 seconds, 4K, nine chapters and 14 click zooms, SHA-256 `7829c870755850477c9d112cee2e3825db555dc8e2dc4d7b8288f099972dffc5`. Its source-challenge, CCTP and independent fictional-request walkthrough is a different test. Its complete record is preserved in `docs/archive/Vittanvaya_VIDEO_DELIVERY.md`, and its full narration remains at `output/demo/Vittanvaya_Narration_Script.md`. Do not relabel it as the new live-case recording.

Final verification: 160 seconds, 3840×2160, H.264/25 fps, 15 click zooms. Full decode, Edge playback/seek and eight-chapter visual QA passed. Narration is 334 words, paced at approximately 118–140 words per minute by chapter. Final SHA-256: `27b377669f15506df1c43333c607df8840b21e59e8c075c8f405aa9b44181671`.

# Vittanvaya final skill and video delivery

**Historical recording edition:** this artifact describes the earlier UI. The interface and live Bitcoin demonstration were updated later on 25 September 2026. A replacement video is explicitly on hold until the user approves the UI; use `docs/UI_REDESIGN_AND_LIVE_DEMO.md` for current scope and the proposed revised sequence.


Verified 25 September 2026, India time. The product was renamed from Custody Atlas to **Vittanvaya / Vittānvaya / वित्तान्वय** before recording. Existing evidence schemas, signing keys, configuration and historical research keep their identifiers for compatibility.

## This request's completed checklist

- [x] Preserve the exact statement, 30 requirement mappings, original 41-section report, all source links, current contracts, tested implementation and full production backlog in one skill.
- [x] Add the complete intended end-to-end solution, architecture, multichain/service strategy, original figure descriptions, five differentiating mechanisms and PPT guidance.
- [x] Apply the researched Sanskrit-derived name to the app brand, icon, browser title, API titles/health and newly generated investigation reports.
- [x] Record nine chapters of actual local UI; show a visible cursor and 14 click-focused zooms; preserve explicit synthetic/proposed labels.
- [x] Deliver native 3840×2160, H.264, 25 fps video at exactly 160 seconds, below the 165-second cap, and its 341-word timed English narration.
- [x] Check full video decoding, browser playback and seek to 150 seconds, representative visuals for all chapters, independent training review, report rendering and skill completeness.

These six delivery checks are separate from the unchanged 12/12 MVP release checks and 50/95 full-product backlog. No additional production or live-integration capability is implied.

## Final artifacts

- `skills/custody-atlas-sih/SKILL.md`: canonical self-contained handover. The stable skill identifier remains for existing discovery. `output/Vittanvaya_COMPLETE_SKILL.md` is an identical shareable copy.
- `output/demo/Vittanvaya_SIH_MVP_4K.mp4`: final film, 160 seconds, 3840×2160, 25 fps, H.264, 35,524,342 bytes. **Silent, narration-ready**, with chapter captions; no synthetic voice or music is implied.
- `output/demo/Vittanvaya_Narration_Script.md`: complete timed English narration, pronunciation and recording instructions; also embedded in the skill.
- `output/demo/Vittanvaya_Training_Investigation.pdf`: actual two-page renamed synthetic investigation export; rendered and inspected.
- `output/demo/Vittanvaya_Training_Evidence.zip`: actual exported signed bundle that the app verified and replayed.
- `output/demo/Vittanvaya_Training_Request.json`: approved fictional recipient/payload package; separate creator/reviewer IDs and equal approved body hashes checked. Status is NOT_SENT / NOT_CONNECTED.
- `output/demo/capture-manifest.json` and `output/demo/video-verification.json`: chapter, click, playback and integrity records.

Video SHA-256: `7829c870755850477c9d112cee2e3825db555dc8e2dc4d7b8288f099972dffc5`.

## What the video establishes

The video records the real application in Microsoft Edge at a native 3840×2160 viewport, with CSS enlarged for readable UI. It is not a sequence of mock screenshots. The application results are generated by the local backend. Presentation-only overlays add title cards, captions and a visible cursor. FFmpeg normalises chapter duration, applies smooth 16% click zooms while keeping the disclosure caption band at its original size, corrects full/limited colour range, and exports an MP4 with chapter metadata and fast-start playback. The film has cuts between chapters and does not represent a benchmark of unedited processing time. No 1080p capture was passed off as native 4K.

The custody and bridge cases were prepared locally with explicit synthetic labels. The bridge proof and request were genuinely reviewed using a separate local reviewer account before filming. The request is export-only. Official SAHYOG dispatch, paid attribution intelligence and validated live provider calls remain unavailable; the closing architecture card is explicitly proposed. The short film does not demonstrate every optional monitoring/feedback or production requirement; the complete scope and gaps remain in the skill.

## Fresh verification after rebranding

TypeScript compilation and Vite production build passed. Python lint passed. `test_report_bundle_replay_and_tamper` passed (one existing Starlette/httpx deprecation warning). `scripts/browser_check.cjs` passed with zero page errors and no mobile horizontal overflow. The nine-chapter capture completed without page errors and successfully exercised source challenge, budgeted expansion, CCTP inspection, signed-bundle replay and reviewed request export. The exported PDF's two pages were visually inspected. FFmpeg decoded the entire final film successfully. Microsoft Edge confirmed 160-second duration, 3840×2160 dimensions, advancing playback, no media error and successful seek to 150 seconds.

The 107-test full suite and four-workflow browser baseline were completed before the presentation-only rename; they remain recorded baseline evidence, not a claim that all 107 tests were rerun for this documentation/video request.

## Reproduce or continue

Capture script: `scripts/record_walkthrough.cjs`. Encoding: `scripts/render_walkthrough.py`. Playback verification: `scripts/verify_walkthrough.cjs`; temporary range-capable preview helper: `scripts/video_preview_server.py`. They use the existing app at 127.0.0.1:8787, local bootstrap credentials read privately, Playwright/Edge, and FFmpeg 7.1 supplied through isolated `tmp/video-deps/imageio_ffmpeg` version 0.6.0. They do not alter application dependency locks. Raw chapters and QA frames are in `tmp/video-raw` and `tmp/video-edited`; these are intermediates, not substitutes for the final MP4.

Run a fresh capture with default settings to create fresh synthetic fixtures. Reusing already-expanded cases can change the visual story; the reuse/coverage-only environment options are editing aids for this recording, not general end-user flows. The preview helper binds only loopback and serves only the finished video. Stop it when verification finishes. The main investigation app remains available independently.

Full design and PPT context: `docs/FINAL_SOLUTION_AND_PRESENTATION.md`, embedded verbatim in the current-state portion of the skill. Never reinterpret a completed media deliverable as completion of the deferred production platform.

# Vittanvaya — timed narration for the 2:40 walkthrough

**Historical recording edition:** this artifact describes the earlier UI. The interface and live Bitcoin demonstration were updated later on 25 September 2026. A replacement video is explicitly on hold until the user approves the UI; use `docs/UI_REDESIGN_AND_LIVE_DEMO.md` for current scope and the proposed revised sequence.


**Video:** `Vittanvaya_SIH_MVP_4K.mp4`. **Length:** 2 minutes 40 seconds. **Language:** English. **Delivery:** calm, clear, approximately 125 words per minute; pause briefly at each chapter. **Pronunciation:** Vittānvaya = “vit-TAAN-va-ya”; VASP = “vasp”; CCTP = “C-C-T-P”. The supplied video is silent and narration-ready, with on-screen chapter captions. Read only the quoted paragraphs, not the production notes or timestamps.

## 00:00–00:09 | Introduction

> Meet Vittanvaya: follow value, verify the link. We connect unknown wallets to evidenced custody boundaries. This demonstration uses synthetic training data.

## 00:09–00:31 | Find the custody boundary

> Investigators need to know which service can act on a wallet trail. Our case workspace follows chronological transfers and stops each branch at its first supported custodian. Here, two custody paths are visible, alongside unresolved branches. The nearestness certificate makes the limits of that answer explicit.

## 00:31–00:49 | Challenge the source

> Our first differentiator is an answer you can challenge. Open the supporting evidence and remove its source as a counterfactual. The result changes to “more evidence required.” Investigators can see which conclusions depend on that source, while retaining the original evidence.

## 00:49–01:14 | Spend the next query carefully

> Next, set a request budget. Vittanvaya prioritises evidence gaps that could change the custody answer, then asks the investigator to review the selected queries. Execution creates a new analysis and preserves its parent. This training expansion makes zero provider calls; live cost savings still require measurement.

## 01:14–01:33 | Cross-chain evidence

> Cross-chain links need protocol evidence, not a guess based on matching amounts. This independently reviewed CCTP training case connects Ethereum and Polygon. The evidence records one USDC burned, a point-zero-one fee, and point-nine-nine minted. Unsupported bridges remain unresolved.

## 01:33–01:56 | Export and independently replay

> The investigation can be exported as a report and signed evidence bundle. We upload that bundle to the verifier: integrity is valid, and replay matches. This makes the result reproducible for another reviewer. A valid signature protects integrity; it does not prove wallet ownership.

## 01:56–02:12 | Prepare the reviewed next action

> The request workflow binds an exact recipient and payload to a separate review. We export the approved training package. Its status remains “not sent,” and SAHYOG is not connected. No real notice or freezing action occurs in this demonstration.

## 02:12–02:23 | Be explicit about coverage

> Six chain adapters are implemented. The coverage screen exposes provider and configuration limits. Live acquisition, licensed attribution data and official integration remain validation gates.

## 02:23–02:40 | The complete proposed solution

> The full design connects authorised intake, chain and intelligence data, custody analysis, reviewed routing and governed feedback. Its production architecture adds durable workers, secure evidence storage and agency identity. Vittanvaya's focus is a defensible next action, with uncertainty and cost kept visible.

## Recording notes

- Start narration at video time zero. The script describes the final edited clip, not the variable-duration raw captures.
- Leave the words “synthetic,” “not sent,” “not connected,” and “proposed” intact. They distinguish a working local MVP from live operational integration.
- The bridge proof and request were genuinely reviewed using a separate local reviewer account before recording. Both the recipient and proof are fictional training material.
- The film shows actual app interaction. Title cards, chapter captions, the visible cursor and postproduction click zooms are presentation elements, not fabricated application results.
- Record your voice in a quiet room, then align each paragraph to the chapter boundary. Do not add unsupported accuracy, recovery, government-endorsement or world-first claims.

<a id="appendix-d"></a>

## Appendix D. Exact current schemas, route inventory, configuration and dependencies

These source excerpts are the inspected contracts at the handover cut-off. They are included to prevent guessing field names, defaults, bounds or routes. If the files change, the new code is authoritative and the handover must be updated. Code blocks are reference material; do not execute an example merely because it appears here.

### Actual API routes

Session/CSRF/role/case protections are implemented in the handlers and helpers described above. The table gives actual registered methods/paths/functions and directly annotated request arguments; it does not invent an official SAHYOG schema. FastAPI's schema is also exposed at `/api/openapi.json`; automatic Swagger/ReDoc routes are disabled and `/api/docs` is the app's own route.

| Method | Route | Handler | Annotated request argument(s) |
|---|---|---|---|

| GET | `/api/health` | `health` | `See handler; path/session parameters only` |

| POST | `/api/auth/login` | `login` | `data: Login, request: Request` |

| GET | `/api/auth/me` | `me` | `request: Request` |

| POST | `/api/auth/logout` | `logout` | `request: Request` |

| POST | `/api/auth/password` | `change_password` | `data: PasswordChange` |

| GET | `/api/users` | `users` | `See handler; path/session parameters only` |

| POST | `/api/users` | `add_user` | `data: UserCreate` |

| GET | `/api/capabilities` | `capability_list` | `See handler; path/session parameters only` |

| GET | `/api/cases` | `cases` | `See handler; path/session parameters only` |

| POST | `/api/cases` | `new_case` | `data: CaseCreate` |

| GET | `/api/cases/{case_id}` | `read_case` | `See handler; path/session parameters only` |

| PATCH | `/api/cases/{case_id}` | `edit_case` | `data: CaseUpdate` |

| GET | `/api/cases/{case_id}/records` | `records` | `See handler; path/session parameters only` |

Analysis creation, reassessment and plan execution use `Idempotency-Key`; authenticated mutations require `X-CSRF-Token`. Recipe/example placeholders are intentional values to obtain from actual responses, not preconfigured credentials.


### `backend/vasp_app/domain.py`

Recorded file SHA-256: `faf1ca53c5d0c2568573be1998b2618c572a770b06597c104313fe68fa5399fb`.

~~~~python
"""Versioned evidence contract. Amounts are integer strings, never floats."""

from __future__ import annotations

from typing import Literal
from pydantic import BaseModel, ConfigDict, Field, model_validator

Chain = Literal["bitcoin", "ethereum", "tron", "bnb", "solana", "polygon"]


class Contract(BaseModel):
    model_config = ConfigDict(extra="forbid")


class Transfer(Contract):
    id: str = Field(min_length=1, max_length=240)
    chain: Chain
    txid: str = Field(min_length=1, max_length=160)
    senders: list[str] = Field(min_length=1, max_length=1000)
    recipient: str
    asset: str
    amount: str = Field(pattern=r"^\d{1,80}$")
    decimals: int = Field(ge=0, le=36)
    timestamp: int = Field(ge=0)
    # Canonical ledger order, where available. Missing order cannot establish same-second causality.
    position: list[int] | None = None
    finality: Literal["finalized", "confirmed", "pending", "orphaned"] = "confirmed"
    success: bool = True
    kind: Literal["native", "token", "internal", "utxo", "bridge"] = "native"
    evidence: list[str] = Field(min_length=1)
    input_outpoints: list[str] = []
    output_index: int | None = Field(default=None, ge=0)
    destination_chain: Chain | None = None
    destination_asset: str | None = None
    bridge_proof: str | None = None

    @model_validator(mode="after")
    def structure(self):
        if self.kind == "utxo" and self.chain != "bitcoin":
            raise ValueError("UTXO records require the Bitcoin chain")
        if len(self.senders) > 1 and self.kind != "utxo":
            raise ValueError("Account transfers have one sender")
        if self.kind == "bridge" and not (
            self.destination_chain and self.destination_asset and self.bridge_proof
        ):
            raise ValueError("Cross-chain links require destination and explicit reviewed proof")
        if self.position is not None and (len(self.position) != 3 or min(self.position) < 0):
            raise ValueError("Position must be [block, transaction index, event index]")
        return self


class Assertion(Contract):
    id: str
    chain: Chain
    address: str
    entity: str = Field(min_length=1, max_length=160)
    category: Literal["exchange", "custodian", "mixer", "bridge", "defi", "unknown"]
    role: Literal["deposit", "hot", "cluster", "contract", "unknown"] = "unknown"
    grade: Literal["reviewed", "provider", "hypothesis"] = "hypothesis"
    source_family: str = Field(min_length=1, max_length=160)
    source: str = Field(min_length=1, max_length=500)
    evidence: list[str] = Field(min_length=1)
    valid_from: int = Field(default=0, ge=0)
    valid_to: int | None = Field(default=None, ge=0)
    risk_tags: list[str] = []

    @model_validator(mode="after")
    def interval(self):
        if self.valid_to is not None and self.valid_to < self.valid_from:
            raise ValueError("Assertion validity interval is reversed")
        return self


class Coverage(Contract):
    chain: Chain
    address: str
    status: Literal["complete", "partial", "unavailable", "not_fetched"]
    reason: str
    pages: int = Field(default=0, ge=0)
    evidence: list[str] = []
    cursor: str | None = None


class BridgeProof(Contract):
    id: str = Field(min_length=1, max_length=160)
    protocol: Literal["cctp-v2"] = "cctp-v2"
    registry_version: Literal["circle-cctp-evm-2026-09-24"] = "circle-cctp-evm-2026-09-24"
    source_chain: Literal["ethereum", "polygon"]
    destination_chain: Literal["ethereum", "polygon"]
    source_txhash: str
    destination_txhash: str
    nonce: str
    source_receipt: dict
    source_block: dict
    destination_receipt: dict
    destination_block: dict
    iris_response: dict
    public_keys: dict
    evidence: list[str] = Field(min_length=1, max_length=20)
    grade: Literal["reviewed", "provider", "hypothesis"] = "hypothesis"
    source_family: str = Field(default="circle-cctp-v2", min_length=1, max_length=160)


class ReconciliationGap(Contract):
    chain: Chain
    address: str
    event_id: str = Field(min_length=1, max_length=240)
    reason: str = Field(min_length=1, max_length=500)
    evidence: list[str] = Field(min_length=1, max_length=40)


class Snapshot(Contract):
    schema_version: Literal["1.0", "1.1", "1.2"] = "1.0"
    mode: Literal["live", "imported", "fixture"]
    origin: str
    collected_at: int
    events: list[Transfer] = Field(default_factory=list, max_length=20000)
    assertions: list[Assertion] = Field(default_factory=list, max_length=20000)
    coverage: list[Coverage] = Field(default_factory=list, max_length=5000)
    # Omitting the empty extension preserves canonical 1.0 snapshots for old bundle replay.
    bridge_proofs: list[BridgeProof] = Field(default_factory=list, max_length=100, exclude_if=lambda v: not v)
    reconciliation_gaps: list[ReconciliationGap] = Field(
        default_factory=list, max_length=20000, exclude_if=lambda v: not v
    )
    limitations: list[str] = []
    # The acquisition window is part of the evidence and cannot silently be extended.
    window_start: int = 0
    window_end: int = Field(ge=1)

    @model_validator(mode="after")
    def unique(self):
        if self.bridge_proofs and self.schema_version not in ("1.1", "1.2"):
            raise ValueError("Bridge proofs require snapshot schema 1.1 or later")
        if self.reconciliation_gaps and self.schema_version != "1.2":
            raise ValueError("Reconciliation gaps require snapshot schema 1.2")
        if self.window_start >= self.window_end:
            raise ValueError("Snapshot window must be increasing")
        for rows in (self.events, self.assertions, self.bridge_proofs):
            if len({r.id for r in rows}) != len(rows):
                raise ValueError("Duplicate evidence identifiers")
        if len({(c.chain, c.address) for c in self.coverage}) != len(self.coverage):
            raise ValueError("Duplicate coverage scope")
        if self.mode != "fixture":
            from .addresses import validate_address

            for e in self.events:
                e.senders = [validate_address(e.chain, a) for a in e.senders]
                e.recipient = validate_address(e.destination_chain or e.chain, e.recipient)
            for a in self.assertions:
                a.address = validate_address(a.chain, a.address)
            for c in self.coverage:
                c.address = validate_address(c.chain, c.address)
            for gap in self.reconciliation_gaps:
                gap.address = validate_address(gap.chain, gap.address)
            if len({(c.chain, c.address) for c in self.coverage}) != len(self.coverage):
                raise ValueError("Duplicate normalised coverage scope")
        return self


class TraceSpec(Contract):
    chain: Chain
    address: str = Field(min_length=1, max_length=160)
    start: int = Field(ge=0)
    end: int = Field(ge=1)
    max_hops: int = Field(default=3, ge=1, le=8)
    max_states: int = Field(default=2000, ge=10, le=10000)
    max_requests: int = Field(default=30, ge=1, le=200)
    asset: str | None = None
    minimum_amount: str = Field(default="0", pattern=r"^\d{1,80}$")

    @model_validator(mode="after")
    def window(self):
        if self.start >= self.end:
            raise ValueError("Start must precede end")
        return self
~~~~

from typing import Literal
from pydantic import Field
from .domain import Contract, TraceSpec, Snapshot, Assertion, BridgeProof


class Login(Contract):
    username: str = Field(min_length=1, max_length=100)
    password: str = Field(min_length=1, max_length=256)


class PasswordChange(Contract):
    current: str = Field(max_length=256)
    password: str = Field(min_length=14, max_length=256)


class CaseCreate(Contract):
    title: str = Field(min_length=3, max_length=160)
    reference: str = Field(min_length=1, max_length=100)
    description: str = Field(default="", max_length=5000)
    classification: Literal["Restricted", "Confidential"] = "Restricted"
    members: list[str] = Field(default_factory=list, max_length=100)


class CaseUpdate(Contract):
    version: int
    status: Literal["Open", "Under review", "Closed"]
    title: str | None = Field(default=None, min_length=3, max_length=160)
    reference: str | None = Field(default=None, min_length=1, max_length=100)
    description: str | None = Field(default=None, max_length=5000)
    classification: Literal["Restricted", "Confidential"] | None = None
    members: list[str] | None = Field(default=None, max_length=100)


class AnalyzeRequest(Contract):
    mode: Literal["live", "imported", "fixture"]
    spec: TraceSpec
    snapshot_id: str | None = None
    fixture_scenario: Literal["custody", "cctp-review"] = "custody"


class SnapshotImport(Contract):
    snapshot: Snapshot
    provenance: str = Field(min_length=10, max_length=2000)


class AssertionCreate(Contract):
    assertion: Assertion
    rationale: str = Field(min_length=10, max_length=3000)


class BridgeCreate(Contract):
    proof: BridgeProof
    mode: Literal["operational", "training"] = "operational"
    rationale: str = Field(min_length=10, max_length=3000)


class Decision(Contract):
    version: int
    decision: Literal["approve", "reject"]
    rationale: str = Field(min_length=10, max_length=3000)


class ChallengeRequest(Contract):
    excluded: list[str] = Field(min_length=1, max_length=100)


class PlanRequest(Contract):
    budget_requests: int = Field(ge=1, le=200)


class PlanExecutionRequest(Contract):
    version: int = Field(ge=1)
    plan_sha256: str = Field(pattern=r"^[a-f0-9]{64}$")


class RecipientCreate(Contract):
    mode: Literal["operational", "training"] = "operational"
    entity: str = Field(min_length=2, max_length=160)
    jurisdiction: str = Field(min_length=2, max_length=100)
    channel: str = Field(min_length=3, max_length=500)
    verification_source: str = Field(min_length=10, max_length=2000)
    signing_public_key: str | None = None
    expires_at: int = Field(gt=0)


class RequestCreate(Contract):
    job_id: str
    candidate_index: int = Field(ge=0)
    recipient_id: str
    type: Literal["disclosure", "preservation", "freezing_review"]
    legal_basis: str = Field(min_length=10, max_length=5000)
    scope: str = Field(min_length=10, max_length=5000)


class FeedbackCreate(Contract):
    request_id: str
    payload: dict
    signature: str = Field(max_length=256)


class WithdrawalCreate(Contract):
    version: int
    rationale: str = Field(min_length=10, max_length=3000)


class WatchCreate(Contract):
    spec: TraceSpec
    interval_minutes: int = Field(default=60, ge=15, le=10080)
    enabled: bool = True


class UserCreate(Contract):
    username: str = Field(pattern=r"^[a-zA-Z0-9_.-]{3,100}$")
    display_name: str = Field(min_length=1, max_length=120)
    password: str = Field(min_length=14, max_length=256)
    role: Literal["investigator", "reviewer", "admin"]
